# AIW capability deep-dives against the reviewed handbooks (each challenged by a skeptic)

Companion to AIW-HANDBOOK-GAP-REVIEW-2026-10-30.md. Hands-on runs used scratch databases outside the repository; the AIW repository was not changed. "Skeptic: DOES NOT HOLD" marks a claim corrected or withdrawn.

**Editor's note (final check).** This file keeps the analysts' and skeptics' words as recorded. One known inconsistency remains: the operability skeptic says there is no error-budget arithmetic, while the deployment skeptic and the code show a time-based availability budget (public/design-reasoning.js:267-270). The summary uses the corrected reading: a time-based budget exists, but no SLI, denominator or burn-rate policy.

## technology-selection

**Question.** Does AIW let the architect select the technology (products, platforms, managed services) that realises each Chapter 6 technology capability? Where do the options come from, how are they assessed and approved, do they conform to standards or a target platform, and what reaches the SDD, compared with the Technology Architecture, Enterprise Component Library Part 3, Target Platform Qualification, Cloud Architecture and Cloud Infrastructure handbooks?

**Answer.** Partly. For each Chapter 7 realization of one or more Chapter 6 capabilities, a person can compare up to 12 options. Each option names a product, version, tier, vendor and operating model and carries typed configuration facts. The person assesses options against the inherited drivers, four fixed criteria, requirement groups and accepted obligations. Recording the selection is person-only, and AIW refuses it while any assessment is stale or a current judgement breaks a hard constraint. Approval evidence attaches separately, and the SDD prints capability → product with the selection state. AIW cannot do these things: offer a governed product or standards catalogue beyond 14 self-managed open-source products (anything else is typed by hand); hold licence, lifecycle (end-of-support), support-contract or vendor-risk data on an option; check a choice against enterprise standards, an approved-product list or a bound target platform (provider, account, region, zones, versions); or record whether the chosen technology was qualified on that target. I confirmed these gaps by running AIW. I recorded Oracle Database 11g R2 as the Bank Payment persistence choice with zero assessments, then recorded my own approval ("me / me / I approve"). The SDD printed "External approval evidence recorded" and "Design basis: Oracle Database … because QD1, QD3, QD6". A Kafka draft preference that was never recorded also became a "Design basis". Sol (the LLM) can only compare two saved options and suggest assessments from the product-mechanism pack (34 traits). It cannot record a selection or an approval.

**Skeptic on the answer.** I reproduced the live-run claims on a fresh server: Oracle recorded with zero assessments, self-approval, the SDD label 'External approval evidence recorded', and Kafka and Oracle printed as 'Design basis'. I also found that self-approval still succeeds with the project's four-eyes setting on, and the approval record stores no actor. Corrections to the direct answer:
(1) It credits Sol with suggesting assessments from the 34-trait mechanism pack. Those suggestions come from deterministic AIW code (product-choice.js:30-40 over product-knowledge.js TRAITS; the count of 34 is right), not from the LLM. Sol's Ch7 role is the two-option comparison prompt (sol-comparison.js:10-30). Sol drafts can add plans and options (architecture-drafter.js:836-841) but can never record or approve (authorship.js:39,57-62).
(2) 'A governed product catalogue beyond 14 products' implies the 14 are governed. They are a discovery list with no approval (technology-catalogue.js:55).
(3) It leaves out two partial capabilities. Governed organisation shared-service dependencies on Ch7 records refuse Retired services, require the version to match and raise drift findings (enterprise-references.js:10-33; guidance-record.js:19). Obligation verification checks give pass / fail / unknown per environment and release (architecture-verification.js:58-76). The answer's 'cannot check against standards' and 'cannot record qualification' should become 'only partly, through these'.
(4) Ch10 does have zones, failure domains, placements and failure simulation (runtime-domain.js:22,152-156,290-302). What AIW lacks is a provider, account, region or version binding, and any reconciliation of Ch10 with the Ch7 option.

**What AIW does today.**

- Ch6→Ch7 trace: a new realization must link at least one existing capability with an allocation scope (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:160). An unrealized capability is an error (:204). The realization inherits applications, requirements, drivers and decisions through those capabilities (realizationSources, :54-55).
- Option record: up to 12 per realization (OPTION_LIMIT :44). Fields: title, product, version, serviceTier, vendor, operatingModel (Self managed / Provider managed / Shared group service), topology, replicaCount 1–1000, failureDomains, workloadProfile, specEvidence, benefits, drawbacks, evidence, sourceUrl (OPTION_FIELDS :172). Up to 64 typed configurationFacts (:175-190). These are 'author-entered assumptions' (:45-47).
- Where options come from: (a) illustrative seeded pairs, Bank Payment reference only, e.g. Kubernetes vs Managed Kubernetes, PostgreSQL vs MongoDB (:119-139); (b) drafter options that name operating models with no product (public/architecture-drafter.js:838-841); (c) an approved, current project-library entry of kind 'technology' adapted into a new option (public/workbench-domain.js:68-75); (d) typing by hand. The catalogue holds 14 products and is a 'discovery projection': 'No product is approved, suitable, deployed, or measured by appearing in this catalogue' (public/technology-catalogue.js:1-25,55).
- Criteria (realizationCriteria :56): every driver that reaches the realization, flagged hard when it is a hard constraint (constraintForce); four fixed criteria, Operational ownership, Recovery and isolation, Lifecycle and exit, and Capacity and cost; requirement groups assessed by coverage (meets / configurable / custom / gap, :52); accepted architecture obligations at weight 0.
- Assessment: effect (needs evidence / supports / trade-off / breaks; 'breaks' only for a constraint driver) or coverage, plus reason, evidence and optional receipts from governed claims or product mechanisms that are re-checked as current (:234-240, cleanAssessmentSupport). A basis fingerprint marks an assessment stale when the configuration or inputs change (:191-196, optionAssessmentCurrent).
- Weighted lean (not a choice): High 3 / Medium 2 / Low 1 per driver, fixed criteria 1. It is shown on recorded judgements and again with suggestions filling gaps, with sensitivity points (public/product-choice.js:1-11). A mechanism counts as context-match only when the option records a version and matching configuration facts (public/product-applicability.js:11-20; public/product-knowledge.js:56-98,104).
- Selection lifecycle: preference → techrealisation.record, which needs reviewed:true, options, a preference, purpose, owner and rationale (selectionPreparation :29-37). It is refused while assessments are stale or a current hard-constraint 'violates' stands (:243). The record snapshots revision, option, inputs, rationale and open finding ids. The selection goes stale when inputs change (selectionCurrent :96). States: none / preferred / recorded / stale / approved (:101-111).
- Approval: techrealisation.approval needs reviewer, authority, evidence and confirmed:true, and only on a current selection (:244). It stops counting when the selection goes stale (approvalCurrent :97). Record and approval are person-only: Sol and agent accounts are refused (authorship.js:39,60).
- Findings: product, version or vendor missing (E); specification incomplete (W); no evidence (E); operations / access / data / resilience / interface / lifecycle plans missing (E each); cost without currency and basis (E); unassessed criterion (W); external approval open (W) (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:206-210). Chapter 8 warns when a participating realization has no current selection (public/interfaces-domain.js:312).
- Cost and exit, partly: annualCost, currency and costBasis on a realization (:145,210). The Workbench 'Compare whole-life cost and exit' tool computes upfront + monthly × horizon + migration + exit in one currency (public/architecture-analysis.js:53-57; public/workbench-ui.js:105). A structured migration or exit procedure is written into lifecyclePlan (public/workbench-domain.js:77-82).
- Organisation library: entries with conditions, limitations, lifecycle text and reviewDue; a release needs independent review of exact source and content (organization-service.js:74-86). Shared services (Shared service / Shared API / Data platform / Enterprise capability) carry a lifecycle of Planned / Active / Deprecated / Retired (organization-service.js:14; public/enterprise-references.js:5). Ch6, Ch7 and Ch10 records can declare a dependency on one (public/enterprise-references.js:4), and the SDD prints those dependencies (public/sdd-architecture.js:886-888).
- SDD: the §4 Technologies line and table column read capability → product with the selection label, plus a source-version warning, stated targets and requirement fit (public/sdd-architecture.js:1303-1330,968-975). The full comparison, rationale and governance go to the Ch7 register, which is an annex (public/review-domain.js:64; public/output-documents.js:63).
- Sol, proposals only: 'Compare products' sends two saved options and up to four quality scenarios with the instruction 'do not invent measured results or make a selection' (public/sol-comparison.js:1-30). Mechanism suggestions become assessments only when a person saves them with receipts (public/product-choice.js).

**Gaps.**

- **[critical | 7 (and SDD §4) | effort S] Ch7 approval is self-attested: no second person, and the SDD reads it as approved**
  - AIW: techrealisation.approval checks only that reviewer, authority and evidence are non-empty and confirmed:true (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:244). It never checks that the approver differs from the person who recorded the selection, unlike the Ch11 baseline approval (public/review-domain.js:262, 'A second signed-in person must record the approval evidence'). Live run on Bank Payment: the same local actor recorded the TR-002 selection and then approval with reviewer 'me', authority 'me', evidence 'I approve'. The Ch7 register printed 'Governance: me · me · I approve'. The SDD component table printed 'TC-002 Oracle Database 11g Release 2 (External approval evidence recorded)'.
  - Handbook: Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › TQ C01 Establish the target qualification boundary › Choose the target by resolving explicit bindings ('a role label must not be presented as a completed human approval'); › Move through separate engineering and qualification states ('Operational approval is an accountable human act within an established organizational process'); Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF Method 12 › Map review responsibilities (a named reviewer per question; 'The review is not a vote that can average away a hard constraint').
  - Recommendation: Apply the independentApproval rule to techrealisation.approval: the approver must be a different signed-in person from whoever recorded the selection. Choose the authority from a project-configured list of authorities (architecture board, security, procurement, licence counsel). In the SDD and register, say 'approval evidence attested by <person>' instead of 'External approval evidence recorded', and never imply that AIW verified it.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds, and the gap is wider than reported. I reran it on a fresh Bank Payment project (scratch DB /home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-tech). The same actor recorded TR-002 = Oracle 11g R2 and then attached the approval 'me/me/I approve'. I then switched the project's four-eyes setting on (workspace.governance fourEyes:true, public/workbench-domain.js:218-223). The same actor still recorded and approved TR-005, and the Ch7 register printed 'Governance: me · me · self'. Four-eyes is enforced only on `confirmed` and proposed→kept transitions (public/provenance.js:125,396-398), and a Ch7 approval changes neither. The approval record also stores no actor at all (technology-realisation-domain.js:244 keeps at, revision, optionId, reviewer, authority and evidence), so the attester can be found only in the change journal (authorship.js:35). On wording: the SDD label reads 'External approval evidence recorded' (:110), not 'approved'. The form's intent is to attest an existing external approval (public/record-fields.js:161), and the selection state is still 'approved' (:106). 'Critical' is arguable, but the gap stands.
- **[high | 7 | effort M] A selection can be recorded with no assessments, and pending hard gates do not block it**
  - AIW: selectionPreparation needs only options, a preference, purpose, owner and rationale (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:29-37). techrealisation.record refuses only stale assessments and a current 'violates' on a hard constraint (:243). Unassessed criteria remain 'criterion' warnings (:207). 'Needs evidence' never blocks. Live run: I recorded TR-002 = Oracle Database 11g R2 with zero assessments against 6 drivers and 4 fixed criteria, and the record succeeded.
  - Handbook: Cloud_Architecture_Handbook › CLD 02 Choosing a service and operating model › Establish hard gates before preferences (filter by region service availability, approved data location, private connectivity, identity integration, recovery support, team support); › Capture the selection without hidden assumptions ('Pending gates mean the design is a candidate. They cannot be silently converted to pass'); Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF-S012 Managed-service composition › Production evidence and conformance ('absent or stale evidence otherwise yields UNKNOWN, never PASS'); Technology_Architecture_Handbook › How to use this volume › The decision workflow.
  - Recommendation: Add gate criteria with three states (pass / fail / pending) for hard constraints, data location, region or service availability, support capability and licence. While any gate is pending or any hard-constraint criterion is unassessed, either refuse the record or store it as a 'conditional selection', and show gate status in the register and SDD. Keep the person-only rule.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds. I confirmed it in code (selectionPreparation :29-35; record :243 refuses only stale assessments and a current hard-constraint 'violates'; 'unknown' never blocks, :207). I confirmed it live: the chosen option had 0 assessments and the record call returned ok. The handbook citations are accurate (Cloud_Architecture_Handbook › CLD 02 › Capture the selection without hidden assumptions: 'Pending gates mean the design is a candidate…'; Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF-S012 › Production evidence and conformance: 'UNKNOWN, never PASS').
- **[high | 7 → SDD §4 | effort S] The SDD prints a draft preference and unassessed drivers as a decided 'Design basis … because QD-x'**
  - AIW: design-questions.js pushes 'decided' whenever selectedOptionId is set, not when a selection is current (public/design-questions.js:106). The SDD then writes 'Design basis: <product> for <component>, because <drivers>' (public/sdd-architecture.js:1016), despite its own comment 'so the document never implies a decision that is not recorded' (:1010-1011). Live run: a Kafka draft preference that was never recorded or assessed produced 'Apache Kafka for APP-004, because QD1, QD2, QD3 (TR-003)'. Oracle, with zero assessments, produced 'because QD1, QD3, QD6'. The 'because' lists the drivers that open the question, not assessed support.
  - Handbook: Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › Principles carried into this edition › AS P12 Keep one model lineage and separate approval ('Documentation completeness, qualification and human approval are separate states'); Technology_Architecture_Handbook › Learning paths and decision record › Reusable decision record (21 Evidence and decision: 'what evidence has actually been observed, what is still assumed').
  - Recommendation: Count a realization answer as decided only when selectionCurrent holds; a preference stays 'draft'. Reword the sentence to 'raised by QD-x' and print 'supported by' only for drivers with a current 'supports' assessment.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds. design-questions.js:106 marks a realization 'decided' whenever selectedOptionId matches an option. The story is built from `because` = the drivers that open the question (design-questions.js:139,143-144), not from assessed support. sdd-architecture.js:1014-1016 then prints it despite the comment at :1010-1011. My independent run reproduced 'Apache Kafka for APP-004, because QD1, QD2, QD3 (TR-003)' from a draft preference that was never recorded. The SDD table on the same page still labels Kafka '(Draft preference)' (sdd-architecture.js:974), so the document contradicts itself.
- **[high | 7 | effort M] No licence, lifecycle (end-of-support), support-contract, vendor-risk or exit data on an option**
  - AIW: OPTION_FIELDS has no licence model or terms, licence-review state, release lifecycle status, end-of-support date, support contract or tier, vendor viability, lock-in or exit fields (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:172). 'Lifecycle and exit' is one generic criterion row (:56). lifecyclePlan is prose at realization level (:145). Live run: Oracle Database 11g Release 2 was accepted, selected and 'approved' with no lifecycle finding. Partial routes exist. A migration or exit procedure can be written into lifecyclePlan as text (public/workbench-domain.js:77-82). Whole-life cost uses upfront + monthly × horizon + migration + exit in one currency (public/architecture-analysis.js:53-57), but it is a Workbench analysis, not a field of the selection. Lifecycle states exist only on organisation shared services (organization-service.js:14).
  - Handbook: Technology_Architecture_Handbook › Learning paths and decision record › Reusable decision record (14 Design parameters incl. versions; 23 Review triggers; 24 Sources and versions); Cloud_Architecture_Handbook › CLD 02 › Start with the work the team can sustain (staffing and incident cost hidden by a lower price); Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF-S012 Managed-service composition › Quality consequences and alternatives ('Portability should have a defined exit scenario and test'); › CIF Method 03 › Apply hard gates before weighted preferences (compare exit requirements).
  - Recommendation: Add typed option fields: licence model, terms reference and review state (pending licence counsel, never set by AIW or Sol); release status and end-of-support date; support arrangement and owner; provider-risk notes; exit scenario and exit test. Add whole-life cost lines (one-time, recurring, migration, exit, operating staff) with a dated basis on the option. Raise a finding when end of support falls before the planned operating horizon.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds for typed fields (OPTION_FIELDS :172). The analyst understates the prose routes, which exist. The fixed 'Lifecycle and exit' criterion explicitly asks about 'Version support, upgrade path, licensing, and a usable exit route' (technology-realisation-domain.js:56). The 'operate' proposal drafts lifecyclePlan text on 'supported versions, upgrade ownership, licence obligations, and an exit path' (:230). The realization's `risks` finding asks for 'supplier risks' (:210). All of these are free text with no dates, states or checks, so the 11g end-of-support case still passes silently.
- **[high | 7 (knowledge, Ch11) | effort L] No enterprise technology standards, approved-product list or radar, and no conformance reading for a chosen product**
  - AIW: The project library accepts kinds 'standard' and 'reference-architecture' (public/workbench-domain.js:180), but shows them only in Chapter 3 (public/workbench-ui.js:103). The guidance-use path refuses them ('Use this entry through its chapter template or architectural reasoning workflow', public/workbench-domain.js:74). No check compares a Ch7 option with any standard. Library content 'enters no score, no hard constraint and no conformance reading' (public/component-library.js:11). An approved 'technology' entry can only add a candidate option (public/workbench-domain.js:73), and organisation shared-service dependencies are declarations (public/enterprise-references.js:4-20). In this release, the only product-related signal AIW can raise is a version mismatch against bundled mechanism documentation (public/technology-catalogue.js:58-63).
  - Handbook: Technology_Architecture_Handbook › Platform automation and change › Platform Services and Paved Paths (supported capability with owner, lifecycle, exceptions); Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF Method 12 › Define a pattern contract (prohibited combinations, review triggers) and › Work an exception request (exception with owner, expiry and removal evidence); Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › TQ C05 Deployment and platform qualification › Bind configuration without inventing a target ('nor treats a familiar cloud service as an approved dependency').
  - Recommendation: Add a governed 'technology standard' record: product, version range, status (adopt / trial / contain / retire), conditions, owner and review date. Publish it only through the four-eyes organisation release; candidate or discovery knowledge must never feed it. Give each selected option a deterministic conformance reading (conforms / exception required / retired / unknown). Add an exception record with owner, expiry and approver, and a Ch11 finding for expired exceptions.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The product-level gap holds: 'standard' and 'reference-architecture' entries appear only in Ch3 (workbench-ui.js:103), the guidance path refuses them (workbench-domain.js:74), and no code compares an option's product or version with a standard. However, 'shared-service dependencies are declarations' undersells what exists. A Ch7 record can depend on a four-eyes-reviewed, signed organisation shared service (enterprise-references.js:4; organization-service.js:80,86). AIW refuses a link to a Retired service (enterprise-references.js:25). It requires the expected version to match the adopted contract (:33). It raises a Ch7 warning, 'Review changed shared-service dependency', when the service is retired or withdrawn or its contract changes (:12-19; guidance-record.js:19). This is a partial, governed lifecycle and version conformance reading for platform services. Deprecated status raises nothing, and none of it applies to the option's product field.
- **[high | 7 ↔ 10 | effort XL] The selected technology is not bound to a target platform, and Ch7 topology is not reconciled with Ch10 placement**
  - AIW: Option topology, replicaCount and failureDomains are free text (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:47,172). Chapter 10 reads realizations as assets (public/runtime-domain.js:30) but never reads selectedOptionId, topology or replicaCount. runtime-domain.js, deploy-model.js and technology-domain.js have no region, availability-zone, account/subscription or network concept. Environment stages are fixed (public/runtime-domain.js:21). The operating-specification export says 'Not an executable or approved infrastructure deployment' (public/workbench-actions.js:36). The catalogue has 24 topology templates (catalogue-index.js) that Ch10 offers no picker for.
  - Handbook: Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › TQ C01 › Choose the target by resolving explicit bindings ('A qualification target is a complete tuple, not an environment nickname': artifact, lock, configuration, database version and roles, topology, network, identity, provider account, secrets, measurement boundary, failure scope); › TQ C05 › Bind configuration without inventing a target (unresolved bindings stay REQUIRED); Cloud_Architecture_Handbook › CLD 05 Region and failure domain selection › Place resources by responsibility and › Record placement assumptions (account, region variable, zone allocation, gate status); › CLD 03 Landing zones and account boundaries › Define the landing zone contract; Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_3_Platform_Deployment_and_Operations › Logical and physical reading guide (technology product and configuration → deployment node → runtime instance).
  - Recommendation: Add a target-binding record per environment: provider, account or subscription, region, zones, cluster or node pool, network segments, product versions, image digest. Each binding is either bound with evidence or unresolved with an owner. Reconcile the selected option's topology, instance count and failure domains with Ch10 placements and zones, and raise a finding on mismatch. Let Ch10 apply catalogue topology templates as reviewed starting points.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The core claim holds: Ch10 never reads selectedOptionId, topology or replicaCount (runtime-domain.js:30 brings realizations in as assets only), and there is no provider, account, subscription, region, version or image-digest binding. One part of the evidence is wrong: 'no availability-zone … or network concept'. Ch10 has zones with kind, owner, failureDomain and isolation (runtime-domain.js:22,305). Plans are placed in zones with an active or standby role and a replica count (:152-156). AIW finds single-domain, missing-standby and shared backup-domain problems (:246-248), and simulates the loss of a zone or failure domain (:290-302; deploy-model.js:1-6). There is networkPolicy text per plan (:115,239) and route and network access per path (:306). These are free text, not provider bindings. The 24 topology templates are confirmed (catalogue-index.js), and Ch10 offers no picker for them.
- **[high | 7 → 10/11 | effort L] No qualification or operational-acceptance state for a selected technology**
  - AIW: Selection states stop at 'approved' (attested external evidence) (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:101-111). specEvidence says 'verification remains separate' (:47), and option 'evidence' is free text (:172). Ch10 design and exercise evidence attaches to runtime plans, not to the product's required behaviours. No ledger records which required behaviours of the chosen product were demonstrated on the target.
  - Handbook: Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › TQ C01 › Move through separate engineering and qualification states (source review → engineering correction → target binding → target execution → evidence review → authorized decision); Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF Method 11 › Map evidence levels; › CIF-S012 › Production evidence and conformance (qualified required behaviours / required behaviours; UNKNOWN, never PASS); Cloud_Architecture_Handbook › CLD 02 › Verify operating capability (cold deploy, credential rotation, rollback, restore handoff with the platform owner).
  - Recommendation: Add a qualification ledger per selected realization. Its rows are required behaviours drawn from criteria, mechanism conditions and obligations. Each row records evidence level, result (pass / fail / unknown) and owner. Show 'selected, unqualified' separately from 'qualified on target X' and feed the result to Ch11. Never set a production-accepted state without every mandatory row passing.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The selection itself has no qualification state (:101-111). But 'no ledger records required behaviours demonstrated on the target' is overstated. Accepted architecture obligations scoped to a realization (:57; architecture-obligations.js:64-70) can carry typed verification checks: metric or assertion, operator, expected value, environmentId, release and procedure. Observations with receipts give pass / fail / unknown / stale, and unknown is the default when nothing is observed (architecture-verification.js:12-25,58-76; obligation states at architecture-obligations.js:11). Ch10 also keeps per-plan runtime evidence (runtime-domain.js:283). The recommended ledger should extend this mechanism rather than duplicate it. A side finding: the Ch7 register always prints 'Verification: pending' for obligations, even ones that are verified (technology-realisation-domain.js:275, a hard-coded string).
- **[medium | 6 → 7 | effort L] Ch6 capability types are closed at 15; many platform components in the handbooks and catalogue have no type**
  - AIW: There are 15 fixed categories (public/technology-domain.js:83-91). The catalogue holds archetypes for secrets manager, object storage, service mesh, schema registry, CI/CD orchestrator, policy engine and vector search (catalogue-index.js), but they become Ch5 component proposals only (public/component-snapshot.js:96-100). Such a capability must be filed under the nearest category and is then checked as that category.
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_3_Platform_Deployment_and_Operations › Reading map (DNS, L4/L7 load balancer, service discovery, mesh proxy, egress gateway, region, zone, cluster, network segment, private connectivity, failover controller, telemetry, alert routing, SLO evaluation, artifact registry, deployment controller, configuration service, feature flags, policy-as-code gate, supply-chain verifier); Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › Component and architecture catalogue (CIF-C001–C036, incl. key management C026, secrets C027, certificates C028, backup vault C035).
  - Recommendation: Replace the closed list with a governed, organisation-released capability-type registry, each type with its own checks and attribute schema. At minimum add key management, secrets, certificates, object storage, DNS and traffic routing, load balancing, artifact registry, CI/CD, policy gate, service mesh and egress.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds. There are 9 + 5 + 1 = 15 categories (technology-domain.js:83-92), enforced as an enum (:316,320). The catalogue's secrets manager, object storage, service mesh, schema registry, CI/CD, policy engine and vector search archetypes exist (catalogue-index.js) and become only Ch5 proposals (component-snapshot.js:96-100). The CIF-C026/C027/C028/C035 headings are verified.
- **[medium | 6/7 | effort L] No provider-neutral attribute schema or fitness tests per capability type**
  - AIW: Configuration facts exist only as product-specific booleans for PostgreSQL, MySQL, MongoDB, Redis, Kafka, RabbitMQ, Kong, NGINX and Kubernetes (public/product-knowledge.js:56-98). Ch6 capabilities carry no capacity, tenancy or SLO attributes; their only numbers are RTO and RPO in minutes. Any other product gets free text only.
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_3_Platform_Deployment_and_Operations › NET 005 API gateway › Physical realization ('Attributes to specify': typed, required, validation and rationale) and › Failure handling and fitness tests (CL-NET-005-T1/T2: Given / When / Then with required evidence); › DEP 002 Availability zone and failure domain (failure-domain key, tolerated domains, survivor capacity).
  - Recommendation: Attach a typed attribute schema and proposed acceptance tests to each capability type. Carry the attributes onto options, check required values, and route the tests to Ch11 verification evidence.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The gap holds, but two details are wrong. First, bundled configuration fields are not only booleans: there are enum and numeric fields too (product-knowledge.js:53-55, e.g. redis.cacheRole :73, kafka.replicationFactor :86, rabbit.members :90). Second, 'any other product gets free text only' is incorrect. Any option, whatever its product, can carry up to 64 typed facts (boolean, number or short string) under any safe key (technology-realisation-domain.js:175-190; architecture-facts.js:6). What is missing is a schema per capability type, required values and acceptance tests.
- **[medium | 7 (knowledge) | effort L] The catalogue and mechanisms cover only 14 self-managed open-source products; there are no managed cloud services**
  - AIW: Catalogue DEFINITIONS lists 14 open-source products (public/technology-catalogue.js:10-25). Seeded managed alternatives read 'Cloud provider · to select' with no product (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:129-137). Any other product becomes a 'custom:' project option with no mechanisms (public/technology-catalogue.js:77). The live Bank Payment reference shows the PostgreSQL 17 seed against PG 18 documentation, so its own mechanisms read 'Unavailable in the stated configuration' (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:120; public/product-knowledge.js:104).
  - Handbook: Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF-S012 Managed-service composition › Logical responsibility and physical realization ('Write a responsibility matrix for selected AWS services and map comparable Azure/GCP capabilities by behavior'); Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_3_Platform_Deployment_and_Operations › NET 005 API gateway › Physical realization (managed Azure API Management vs self-hosted gateway options).
  - Recommendation: Let an organisation publish governed product profiles (behaviours, documented versions, configuration fields, sources) through the reviewed release path instead of code changes. That copy is not linked to the Ch7 selection, though, so it is a second record, not a fix.

But 'the lean shows no uncertainty' is wrong. weighOptions computes low and high bounds over unrecorded judgements and a dominance check that holds across them (decision-reading.js:52-75), and the lean reports sensitivity points (product-choice.js:9-10). ## component-selection

**Question.** Does AIW let the architect SELECT COMPONENTS that realise the architecture? This covers the path from Chapter 4 responsibilities to Chapter 5 components: whether a component can come from a library or pattern catalogue, what the picker shows, how the choice is recorded and traced, whether alternatives are compared and rejected ones kept, and what reaches the SDD. It also sets out what AIW lacks to support the handbooks' method for deriving and selecting components.

**Answer.** Partly. AIW records which component realises each responsibility: a Chapter 4 mapping, then a Chapter 5 allocation with a role and a scope. Chapter 5 also has a "From the library" picker. But that picker can only choose from two sources. One is the catalogue's 20 component archetypes, which are platform capabilities (API gateway, broker, data stores), carry templated text and have no live source claims. The other is snapshots of components filed from projects. None of the 120 Enterprise Component Library profiles, the 100 Component Dictionary entries or the 20 Application Architecture Master components is imported, and a component can name only the 19 SA Playbook styles and patterns, not the 240 catalogue patterns (tested on the running app: refused). The picker does not start from the responsibility or its drivers. Each card shows a name and id (or a purpose and counts), with no fit to quality attributes, no hard-constraint check, no consequences, alternatives or evidence. The archetype's own quality impacts, risks, failure modes and alternatives are dropped when the proposal is built. Accepting a proposal is a person's reviewed act, and the origin (release id and record hash) stays on the component. But Chapter 5 records no selection decision with eligible and rejected candidates, the origin never reaches the SDD, and SDD section 4 does not print the component's rationale. A real compare-and-choose that keeps the rejected options exists only for five fixed mechanism topics, where the composition task writes every option as a Chapter 3 alternative, and for one guided cohesive-versus-separate boundary task. Sol can explain, compare recorded alternatives and challenge, but it never selects. Governance holds in that library content never scores, but unreviewed library drafts can still be proposed into a design.

**Skeptic on the answer.** The overall answer stands: AIW selects components only partly. Five points need correcting. (1) 'A real compare-and-choose that keeps the rejected options exists only for five fixed mechanism topics … and one guided task' is wrong. Architecture design tasks also cover interaction (architecture-design.js:137-156; interaction-knowledge.js:4-7), reads (read-design.js; read-design-knowledge.js:13), persistence (persistence-design.js; persistence-knowledge.js:17) and governed released design concerns (architecture-design.js:29,41-42,133). Each writes every option as a Chapter 3 alternative with one chosen. Model Alternatives also let a person save, compare (no ranking) and set aside, with a reason, alternative edits of existing components (model-alternatives.js:19-21,71; SDD sdd-architecture.js:695). None of these selects from a component library. (2) 'The origin never reaches the SDD' is wrong. The SDD's Appendix D Component specifications annex prints the component's Source line, which for an archetype proposal reads 'Catalogue archetype <id> · <release> · record <hash prefix>' (component-snapshot.js:115; component-spec-document.js:103). It is editable free text, not the structured c.library, and the section 4 body omits it. The rationale is printed in neither. (3) The archetype's 'alternatives' cannot be 'dropped': all 20 archetypes have empty alternatives and complements, and their quality impacts, risks and failure modes are templated. Little substantive content is lost; the source content is what is missing. (4) 'Sol … never selects' is right about selection. But Sol, the model, also drafts components per module with responsibilities, an architectural solution and drivers served with reasons, as proposals (architecture-contract.js:83-86,741). (5) Add: 8 of the 20 archetypes do not even claim applicationRealization as a stage, yet the Chapter 5 picker offers all 20 (component-library.js:50). The organisation library offers draft entries despite AIW's own text saying entries reach other projects only through a signed release (component-library-ui.js:31,46).

**What AIW does today.**

- Chapter 4 mapping stage asks 'Which component will realise each responsibility' (public/work-frame.js:64-67). logical.mapping links a responsibility to an existing physical candidate or creates one with newComponent (public/logical-domain.js:195-203).
- Chapter 5 component editor has three steps (Component, Traceability, Review). Traceability lists every responsibility as a checkbox showing its boundary and requirement ids, with a realises/supports role and an implementation scope (public/realisation-ui.js:53,61-63,78-87). These are saved as logical.mappings with a snapshot of the responsibility (public/realisation-domain.js:256-264).
- 'From the library' button (public/realisation-ui.js:123) opens a modal listing three groups: project library entries that are not deprecated, organisation entries that are not withdrawn, and the 20 catalogue archetypes. Filtering is a plain text search only (public/component-library-ui.js:31,34,64-71). An archetype card shows only its name, its id and 'descriptive knowledge, never a score' (public/component-library-ui.js:37). An entry card shows purpose, status and counts of interfaces, data and needs (public/component-library-ui.js:36; public/component-snapshot.js:49-51).
- Proposal path: the choice appears as an unsaved ghost on the model next to the selected responsibility (or the first one not yet realised). The person reviews and edits it in the component form. It is saved through realisation.candidate only with reviewed:true, as status 'candidate' with origin library or archetype (public/realisation-ui.js:108-114; public/realisation-domain.js:332-342). The component keeps a library reference: scope, archetypeId, releaseId and recordSha256, or entryId, revision and contentHash (public/component-library.js:63-71). It also keeps the proposed contracts, needs and obligations (public/component-library.js:54-61). Tested on the running app: APP-007 was saved with releaseId AKR-0.10.73.5 and the record hash.
- Archetype proposal builder: maps summary to purpose, required componentKit parts to boundary, interfaceKit to inputs, outputs and contracts, capability or control parts to technology needs, and prerequisites, applicabilityRules and exclusions to assumptions. The kind comes from a regex on the archetype id. It carries the knowledge authority as descriptive, with scoringCalibrated false (public/component-snapshot.js:62-68,98-120).
- 'Add to the library' files one component's specification snapshot into the project library, or into an organisation library. An organisation entry needs a different reviewer and a signed release (public/component-library-ui.js:41-55; organization-service.js:78-81,85-87).
- Pattern picker on a component offers the SA Playbook's 5 styles and 14 patterns by name only (public/realisation-ui.js:76). The domain also accepts the 56 tactics and refuses any other id (public/realisation-domain.js:38,240). Each component can also hold up to 24 quality property rows (concern, statement, mechanism, source) and a free-text architectural solution (public/realisation-ui.js:74-77; public/realisation-domain.js:219-223).
- 'serves' (drivers served, each with a reason) is stored and validated (public/realisation-domain.js:198-218) and printed in the SDD's high-level line (public/sdd-architecture.js:921-952). It has no field in the editor (public/record-fields.js:144). Tested on the running app: accepted when sent as a command payload.
- Drivers reach a component through its responsibilities and requirements (public/realisation-domain.js:59-64). The inspector lists upstream drivers with their targets and decisions with acceptance state (public/realisation-ui.js:134-138). The component fingerprints these inputs and raises a source-change finding when they change (public/realisation-domain.js:73-79).
- Pattern DNA design questions land on components (read-path, persistence, partitioning, handoff, edge, protected-call, structure), each with options, products and a caution. The DNA never chooses an option (public/pattern-dna.js:7-12). An open question raises a warning, and a question counts as answered by a task, decision, need, selection or contract (public/design-questions.js:88-122; public/realisation-domain.js:168). The clinic reference project shows 7 such warnings.
- Composition tasks cover five fixed topics: delivery, resilience, distribution, partitioning and structure. Each compares options with applicability checks and blockers (public/composition-knowledge.js:30-89,143-168). Applying one writes a Chapter 3 decision with every option as an alternative and the chosen one selected. It also writes new role responsibilities, components (or reuses ones the person selects), interactions, capabilities and a Chapter 7 plan (public/composition-design.js:23,31-32,82-83).
- Guided design task compares a cohesive application boundary with a separate coordination boundary, each with prerequisites, benefits, trade-offs, failure and what to avoid. It is previewed as a model alternative, and set-aside drafts are archived with a reason (public/design-planner.js:25-28; public/design-task-domain.js:9,44-50).
- Chapter 3 alternatives can name components (componentIds, existing components only), a pattern (free text with a datalist of catalogue names) and a rejection reason (public/record-fields.js:131,429-435; public/decisions-domain.js:183). The decision reading is deterministic, and SDD section 3 prints why each alternative was not chosen (public/sdd-architecture.js:739).
- Co-design dialog offers three fixed rule-based implementation patterns (review queue, idempotent receiver, outcome recovery) (public/realisation-ui.js:164-170). In a blank project they become generic proposals (public/realisation-domain.js:290-324).
- Drafters: the journey drafter creates one component per unrealised responsibility, with the kind taken from a regex on its title (public/journey-drafter.js:333-344). The grouped architecture drafter is deterministic and labelled sol-draft. It proposes one component per functional area plus a fixed list of cross-cutting components, naming the drivers each serves (public/architecture-drafter.js:1-15,34-37).
- Sol: the 'Compare alternatives' preset ends 'Leave the selection to the architect', and 'Explore relevant patterns' explains eligible patterns (public/sol-question-presets.js:5,8). The design starters ('Why this kind of component here?') explain only and 'propose nothing as decided' (public/intelligence-context.js:129-138). The catalogue reading in Sol's panel lists the archetypes, each with 'Propose into this project' (public/component-library-ui.js:82-92).
- Output: SDD section 4 lists the styles and patterns used, from chosen alternatives and component patternIds (public/sdd-architecture.js:834-837). It prints a high-level line per component (kind, purpose, responsibilities, serves) and a specification table (public/sdd-architecture.js:921-982). The per-component specification shows 'Why this component' and provenance (source text, origin) (public/component-sheet.js:98,252-255). The chapter export prints rationale, source and origin (public/realisation-domain.js:377).
- Knowledge posture: library and catalogue content 'enters no score, no hard constraint and no conformance reading' (public/component-library.js:11). No component content from the handbooks exists in the tree: a search for CL-APP, SCD-C0 and AAP-C0 found none.

**Gaps.**

- **[critical | Chapter 5 / Knowledge | effort XL] No governed component library to select from: the handbooks' 240 component definitions are not imported, and the 20 archetypes that are offered sit at the wrong level and carry templated text**
  - AIW: CANNOT. The only catalogue content in the Chapter 5 picker is the 20 records of type component-archetype (public/component-library.js:50; public/catalogue-index.js). All are platform capabilities named ARCH-*-CAPABILITY (API gateway, broker, relational, document and vector data, identity provider and so on), not application responsibilities. Their records are editorial: in AKR-0.10.73.5, 327 of 328 records say 'descriptive-and-editorial-composition-only' and every record has liveImmutableSourceClaimCount 0 (public/brain-catalogue.js, knowledgeAuthority and lineageSummary). AIW's own curation admits that the archive content is 'the catalogue's template' (public/catalogue-curation.js:6-7). The other source is snapshots of components filed from projects (public/component-library-ui.js:41-55). A search for CL-APP, SCD-C0 and AAP-C0 found none in the tree.
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › How to use this component library › Read for the decision you need to make: 'a component as a design commitment: a responsibility, a boundary, a contract and an operational obligation'. The three parts hold 120 profiles in 15 families (APP, BUS, INT, MSG, RES; DAT, DST, AIN, SEC, PRO; RUN, NET, DEP, OBS, DEL). Software_Design_Component_Dictionary_Master › How to use the component dictionary: 'one hundred software component definitions … The structured JSON catalogue mirrors the readable definitions and can seed a governed architecture workbench. Importing it does not automatically approve a component'. Application_Architecture_Master › Component and architecture catalogue (AAP-C001 to AAP-C020, each with a 'Component contract').
  - Recommendation: Define a governed component-profile pack with stable id and version, semantic kind and lifecycle stage. Each profile carries: use-when and choose-another conditions, credible alternatives with their selection condition and cost, responsibility and boundary, owned information, invariants, semantic ports, physical realisation options (topology, configuration, operating cost), attributes to specify, NFR consequences, metric contract, fitness and conformance tests, failure scenario, related profiles, and source passages with hashes. Import the ECL, SCD and AAM content through the existing source, claim, four-eyes review, release and pin lifecycle (knowledge-governance.js:137-251), with the licence reviewed. Keep it descriptive: it never scores. Re-level the 20 archetypes as Chapter 6 capability profiles rather than Chapter 5 application components. Record the real review status of the imported content; do not mark it approved.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The gap holds, and the evidence is stronger than stated. The catalogue holds exactly 20 component-archetype records (catalogue-index.js, release AKR-0.10.73.5), all ARCH-*-CAPABILITY. All 20 have templated summaries ('… normalized as an AIW component-archetype record …'). 327 of 328 records are activeUse 'descriptive-and-editorial-composition-only', and all 328 have liveImmutableSourceClaimCount 0 (brain-catalogue.js). Extra evidence: 8 of the 20 archetypes do not list 'applicationRealization' in their own applicableStages (DOCUMENT-DATA, VECTOR-SEARCH, POLICY-ENGINE, SERVICE-CATALOG, EVENT-ROUTER, MODEL-GATEWAY, SERVICE-MESH, DISTRIBUTED-LOCK). The Chapter 5 picker still offers all 20, because COMPONENT_ARCHETYPES filters by type only (public/component-library.js:50). The categories are also wrong: Relational Data is filed under 'security' and Document Data under 'observability'. No CL-APP, SCD-C0 or AAP-C0 content exists anywhere in the tree. A small citation fix: the governance module is public/knowledge-governance.js, not a root-level knowledge-governance.js. The handbook counts check out (ECL: 3×40 profiles in 15 families; SCD: 100 SCD-C entries; AAM: AAP-C001 to C020). So do the quotes.
- **[high | Chapter 4 → Chapter 5 | effort L] The picker does not start from the responsibility and its drivers: it shows no fit, no constraint check, no consequences, no alternatives and no evidence**
  - AIW: PARTLY. 'From the library' lists every entry and archetype and filters by free text only (public/component-library-ui.js:64-71). An archetype card shows name and id only (public/component-library-ui.js:37). The proposal attaches to whichever responsibility is selected, or the first unrealised one (public/realisation-ui.js:113). Building the proposal drops the archetype's qualityImpacts, risks, failureModes, counterfactualExplanation, alternatives, complements, conflicts and fitnessTests (public/component-snapshot.js:98-120 reads only summary, problem, kits, obligations, prerequisites, applicabilityRules and exclusions). The Chapter 5 pattern picker shows names only (public/realisation-ui.js:76), even though Playbook entries carry per-attribute effects, text and an example (public/playbook-knowledge.js). Driver context exists elsewhere: the inspector (public/realisation-ui.js:134-138) and design questions (public/design-questions.js:88-122). It is not used to filter or explain candidates.
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Derive components from problems and quality drivers › Resolve hard constraints before preferences: 'A candidate with an unknown answer remains unproven. Do not translate unknown into a neutral numeric score. Preference comparison begins only after eligible candidates have satisfied the hard conditions'. ECL Part 1 › Build useful architecture views from one model › Apply a component in a workbench: 'A preview should show the selected responsibility, recommended relationships, required attributes, interfaces and new obligations'. ECL Part 1 › APP 001 Channel experience › Problem and selection lays out Symptoms, Forces, Use when, Choose another approach when, and Alternatives with selection condition and cost.
  - Recommendation: Start selection from a responsibility. Read the drivers that reach it (driversForResponsibility) and its constraint drivers, and list candidate profiles matched by stage and semantic kind. Show deterministic eligibility first: pass, fail or unknown against the project's hard constraints, with unknown never treated as neutral. Then show the profile's descriptive content: use-when and choose-another, alternatives, quality effects labelled uncalibrated, operating obligations and evidence status. Do not rank or score. The person chooses. Sol may suggest candidates, with receipts, as proposals only.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The core claim holds. The picker filters by free text only (public/component-library-ui.js:67). An archetype card shows only its name and id (component-library-ui.js:37). The default allocation is the selected responsibility or the first unrealised one (public/realisation-ui.js:113). One sub-claim overstates the loss: 'drops the archetype's … alternatives, complements, conflicts'. Every one of the 20 archetypes has alternatives=[] and complements=[], and 18 have conflicts=[]. qualityImpacts are two templated rows each ('has a context-dependent impact on … AIW requires scenario-level calibration'). risks and failureModes are generic ('Misapplying X can add complexity…') (brain-catalogue.js). So archetypeProposal (public/component-snapshot.js:98-120) loses almost no real selection content. The real deficit is the missing source content (Gap 1), not the builder discarding it.
- **[high | Chapter 3 / Chapter 5 | effort L] No component-selection decision: Chapter 5 records no eligible or rejected candidates, and rejected options are kept only for five fixed mechanism topics**
  - AIW: PARTLY. Accepting a library proposal writes one candidate component (public/realisation-domain.js:337-342). There is no record of the other candidates considered or why they were rejected. A Chapter 3 alternative can name only components that already exist (public/decisions-domain.js:183; tested on the running app: 'Choose existing components: obj-999 is not in this project'). So each rejected candidate must first be created in Chapter 5, where it raises findings. Alternative componentIds are not printed in the SDD or MADR (no componentIds in sdd-architecture.js, sdd-document.js or madr.js). The exceptions are composition tasks, which write every option as an alternative and select one (public/composition-design.js:82-83), but only for delivery, resilience, distribution, partitioning and structure (public/composition-knowledge.js:30-89). The guided task compares only cohesive against separate boundaries (public/design-planner.js:25-28). Chapter 5 cannot compare physical realisation options for an application component (the Option 1 and Option 2 pattern). Product comparison exists only for capabilities in Chapter 7.
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Reusable decision and evidence templates › Component selection decision (TPL-ADR) lists: problem and triggering quality scenario, hard constraints with evidence, eligible alternatives, preference scale and evidence, selected responsibility and boundaries, physical realisation and topology, consequences and operating cost, required tests, review trigger and rollback plan. Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Compare feasible designs and record the decision: 'Evaluate mandatory conditions first … Record the rejected option fairly … The review trigger should be observable'. Software_Design_Component_Dictionary_Master › SCD-CH02 › Choose modularity and deployment boundaries › Compare the physical options.
  - Recommendation: Add a component-selection decision linked to one or more responsibilities. Its alternatives are candidate profiles or realisation options and do not need to exist as components first. Reuse the deterministic decision reading: hard-constraint ruling, dominance, sensitivity. Require a rejection reason for each alternative and a review trigger. Accepting the decision instantiates only the chosen profile, through the reviewed realisation.candidate path, and links the component to the decision. Print the selected and rejected candidates for each responsibility in SDD sections 3 and 4.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — No component-selection decision exists, and no library candidate is ever recorded as rejected, so the gap holds. But 'only five fixed mechanism topics' undercounts. Architecture design tasks also compare options and write every option as a Chapter 3 alternative with one chosen, then create or reuse components, for: interaction (request-reply vs durable queue: interaction-knowledge.js:4-7, architecture-design.js:137-156); reads (direct read vs cache-aside: read-design-knowledge.js:13, read-design.js); persistence (relational vs aggregate document: persistence-knowledge.js:17, persistence-design.js); and governed released design concerns ('knowledge:<id>': architecture-design.js:29,41-42,133; domain-knowledge.js:115-116). Separately, Model Alternatives let a person save named ALT bundles of edits to existing Chapter 4–7 records, including components. They can be compared two at a time without ranking, set aside with a reason, and applied with a named reviewer (model-alternatives.js:19-21,71). Their saved reasoning prints in the SDD (sdd-architecture.js:695). They accept only existing records ('Coordinated alternatives support existing records in Chapters 4–7'). None of these routes starts from a library or catalogue candidate. The claim that alternative componentIds are missing from the SDD and MADR is correct. They do print in the Chapter 3 chapter export (decisions-domain.js:251, 'Components:').
- **[high | Chapter 5 / Chapter 3 | effort M] The trace from responsibility to component, pattern or profile, and driver is broken in the model**
  - AIW: PARTLY. (a) patternIds accepts only Playbook ids, and the 240 catalogue patterns are refused (public/realisation-domain.js:38,240; tested on the running app: 'PAT-CIRCUIT-MEDIATED-INTEGRATION is not in this project'). (b) The library or archetype reference (c.library) is kept (public/realisation-domain.js:252-254) but read by no other module: no finding, view or document uses it. A realization should link back to the responsibility it implements … A recommendation without its triggering force and obligations is incomplete knowledge'. Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Derive component responsibilities from drivers: 'An architect should be able to point from each attribute to the scenario it is intended to satisfy and to the experiment that could refute the assumption'.

(b) overstates. c.library is read by the Chapter 5 UI: the inspector prints 'From the catalogue archetype <title>' for a saved component, and the review form and the ghost model node show the origin too (realisation-ui.js:38,80,101,145). What is true: no finding or SDD body reads the structured c.library, and no version or pin is checked. ## deployment-architecture

**Question.** Does AIW let the architect design and SELECT A DEPLOYMENT ARCHITECTURE? This covers Chapter 10 (environments, zones, plans, placements, paths, recovery, operate and release) and its inputs from Chapters 6 and 7: topology, the environment chain, reference blueprints and how one is chosen, HA/DR, scaling, network security zones, CI/CD and IaC, observability, operational acceptance, and what the Model draws and the SDD prints. Compared with VPC, Cloud Architecture, Cloud Infrastructure Master, Dev/QA/Banking Production Environment, DevOps Implementation, DevOps Master, Enterprise Component Library Part 3 and Target Platform Qualification.

**Answer.** Only partly. AIW lets the architect describe one deployment and check it. That means environments, named zones whose failure domain is free text, one runtime plan per environment and part (min/max replicas, a recovery strategy from a fixed list, assumed recovery-time and data-loss minutes, operating text), placements, runtime paths, and about 30 deterministic completeness and failure-domain checks. It can also walk a zone failure, and the SDD prints a placement line per component plus one environment's deployment diagram. AIW CANNOT select a deployment architecture. Chapter 10 has no candidate or option record, no reference-blueprint library, no step that applies hard gates before preferences, and no recorded selection. Chapter 3 decisions cannot target Chapter 10 records, model alternatives stop at Chapter 7, and the catalogue's 24 topology templates are generated boilerplate that no record can apply. It also cannot model physical topology (region, availability zone, account, VPC, subnet, cluster, edge), an environment chain with parity and promotion, CI/CD or IaC, network flow matrices, data residency against location, DR tiers or SLOs. These are prose fields or absent. Sol (the LLM) only drafts proposals and a person decides. Its reference-journey draft even creates one 'production design' environment per component, and that alone raises missing-dependency errors.

**Skeptic on the answer.** The 'Only partly' verdict is right, but four of the CANNOT claims are overstated. (1) 'No candidate or option record … and no recorded selection' is wrong. Saved design alternatives accept Chapter 10 commands. AIW compares 2 to 6 of them on diff, capacity (failed replicas, downstream limit) and cost. A person, never Sol, records 'accepted' or 'not selected' with a reason (workbench-domain.js:51-58, :145-151, :166-172; authorship.js:39), and the SDD prints them in section 3 (sdd-architecture.js:693-711). I verified this in node on a scratch copy of bank-payment. What is truly missing is a governed blueprint source, driver or hard-gate evaluation of the alternatives, and a deployment-approach record that plans reference. The 'reference-architecture' and 'pattern' library kinds exist (workbench-domain.js:180) but cannot be applied (workbench-domain.js:74). (2) 'No environment chain with parity' needs qualifying. A pairwise 'Compare environments' diff of 13 plan fields and the placements exists in Chapter 10 (architecture-analysis.js:43-47; workbench-ui.js:107). The grouped drafter proposes Production, Pre-production and Recovery environments that each hold all parts (architecture-drafter.js:984-990). Promotion order, an environment contract and stage-aware checks are still absent. (3) 'SLOs … absent' is wrong. Availability drivers with windows give computed unavailability (error) budgets, checked per part against placement and recovery time (design-reasoning.js:248-293; desk-vitals.js:127-138). SLI definitions, alert routing and a telemetry inventory are what is missing. (4) Survivor capacity after a zone loss is computed (desk-capacity.js:34, :171-175, :328; architecture-analysis.js:48-52), and so is quorum survival (desk-vitals.js:90-100). The review desk drafts per-part HA postures (pair, quorum, spread, single) as Chapter 10 proposals a person confirms (desk-fixes.js:51-117). One minor label error: the four stages are 'Production design', 'Pre-production design', 'Recovery design' and 'Development design' (runtime-domain.js:21).

**What AIW does today.**

- Chapter 10 has three Work stages: Placement ('Where will this workload run, with what capacity and independent failure domains?'), Recovery and Operate & release (work-frame.js:95-99; runtime-ui.js:95-100).
- runtime.environment has a title, purpose, owner, basis, assetIds and a stage limited to four labels: Production, Pre-production, Recovery and Development design (runtime-domain.js:21; record-fields.js runtime.environment entry, lines 189-204). No rule reads the stage: it appears only at runtime-domain.js:310 (validation of the value) and :343 (export).
- runtime.zone has a kind (hosting or access), a free-text failureDomain of up to 180 characters, isolation prose, an owner and the trust boundaries it holds (record-fields.js runtime.zone). Zone independence is decided by comparing failure-domain strings case-insensitively (runtime-domain.js:246-247).
- runtime.plan is one per environment and asset. It holds minReady/maxReplicas, prose scalingPolicy, readiness and networkPolicy, a stateMode, a recoveryStrategy from {Restart, Failover, Restore, Provider recovery} (runtime-domain.js:26-27), recoveryMinutes/lossMinutes as assumptions, fencing, backupPlan and backupZoneId, and prose monitoring, runbook, rollout, rollback and releaseReference. It also holds target rows tied to Chapter 2 drivers (driver-links.js:22-49) and batch windows.
- runtime.placement records active or standby copies with a replica count per zone. runtime.path is one per environment and contract, with mode, start-up need, crossed trust boundaries and failure response. runtime.evidence records design or exercise evidence per plan with a reviewer and reference (record-fields.js runtime.placement/path/evidence; runtime-domain.js:334).
- With seeding on, AIW proposes a plan for every asset in an environment and a path for every contract that touches it (runtime-domain.js:74-99). Live check: adding a 'Clinic QA' environment through /api/commands produced a proposed RUN-007.
- runtimeFindings runs deterministic checks (runtime-domain.js:207-275). Examples: zone owner, failure domain and isolation (:227); missing supporting plan (:224); single-domain warning (:246); Failover without a standby in another failure domain (:247); a path crossing a trust boundary without naming a control (:261); state protection; backup in another domain; unbound Chapter 9 controls; capacity basis; operations prose present.
- Failure walkthrough (runtime.probe/simulation) for one zone or one failure domain, labelled simulated, with promotion, health and consistency 'unproven' (runtime-domain.js:290-303). The Model's 'If a zone fails' view reuses simulateRuntime (deploy-model.js:1-7).
- The Model draws a per-environment Deployment picture: trust boundaries, zones, placements, what each part stands on, and numbered runtime paths (notation-model.js:57, :459-490). It also draws a Technology usage picture and gives integrity insights (deploy-model.js).
- Chapter 11 detects two anti-patterns from Chapter 10. ANTI-SINGLE-POINT-OF-FAILURE: a part carrying a High or Medium driver with one replica and no standby. ANTI-OBSERVABILITY-AS-AFTERTHOUGHT: no monitoring text recorded (design-spec.js:237-281). The ARS gate needs each driver to be carried by a runtime plan (readiness-model.js:223-232).
- Chapter 11 typed verification checks are bound to an existing Chapter 10 environment and a release, and the observed-evidence environment and release must match (architecture-verification.js:24, :49-50).
- SDD output: a 'Placement:' line per component (sdd-architecture.js:1028-1030); one C4 deployment diagram for one environment (sdd-architecture.js:1450). Live check: the bank-payment SDD shows only ENV-001. The executive edition's 'Where it runs' gives counts only (sdd-document.js:370-375). There is also the 'Deployment and runtime register' chapter export (runtime-domain.js:343; output-documents.js:69).
- Chapter 6 holds failure domains, a continuity enum and a capability failure simulation (technology-domain.js:378-410). Chapter 7 options hold topology, replicaCount and failureDomains as free text, explicitly 'separate from the Chapter 10 deployment' (technology-realisation-domain.js:45-47).
- A deployment choice can be framed only indirectly, as a generic Chapter 3 decision. The Playbook tactic picker offers D-DP-1 CI/CD, D-DP-2 IaC, D-DP-4 Blue-Green, D-DP-5 Canary, D-AV-1 Failover and D-AV-5 Geographic Distribution (playbook-knowledge.js TACTICS). But every alternative must name Chapter 4 responsibilities (decisions-domain.js:125), and a runtime plan does not reference a decision (runtime-domain.js:68).
- Sol and the rules, all as proposals only. Three rule-based co-design ghosts: standby isolation, controlled restoration, owned operating path (runtime-ui.js:123; runtime-domain.js:286-288). Journey drafter: a plan, zone and placement per component (journey-drafter.js:433-450). Grouped drafter: 'one set of environments, one zone each, a placement per component with copies not stated' (architecture-drafter.js:45-46). The field spark drafts prose only, never measured values.
- Reuse of a deployment arrangement is possible only by copying a whole project as a template, with reviews and confirmations reset (project-lifecycle.js:6-14).
- The component library holds only application-component snapshots. Catalogue archetype proposals land only in Chapter 5 (component-library.js:1-11; component-snapshot.js:1-13).
- The catalogue (AKR-0.10.73.5) holds 24 topology templates, including TPL-MULTI-REGION-ACTIVE-ACTIVE, TPL-CELL-BASED-WORKLOAD and TPL-GITOPS-DELIVERY-PIPELINE, plus deployment patterns such as PAT-AVAILABILITY-ZONE-DISTRIBUTION, PAT-LANDING-ZONE and PAT-BLUE-GREEN-DEPLOYMENT (catalogue-index.js). All 24 templates carry the generated summary 'normalized as an AIW topology-template record' (brain-catalogue.js). Apart from catalogue-curation.js (:37-38), no code references a TPL- id. AIW's curation itself marks PAT-DISASTER-RECOVERY, BACKUP-AND-RESTORE, FAILOVER, GEO-REDUNDANCY and ACTIVE-PASSIVE as template content with no RTO, RPO or restore path (catalogue-curation.js:28-32).
- Live run on a scratch database. The bank-payment reference has 1 environment, 2 zones whose failure domains read 'Primary site · assumption' and 'Recovery site · assumption', 14 plans all with min/max 1 and recovery Unspecified, and 3 placements: 142 Chapter 10 findings. The clinic-appointments reference has 6 'production design' environments, one per component, with 6 zones and 6 single placements: 102 findings, including 6 missing-dependency errors.

**Gaps.**

- **[critical | 10 (with 3 and 7) | effort XL] [CANNOT] No way to propose, compare and select a deployment architecture or reference blueprint**
  - AIW: Chapter 10 records have no option, alternative or selection fields (record-fields.js runtime.* entries, lines 189-204). Chapter 3 alternatives must name Chapter 4 responsibilities (decisions-domain.js:125), and a plan reaches decisions only through its asset (runtime-domain.js:68). Model alternatives refuse anything outside 'existing records in Chapters 4–7' (model-alternatives.js:21). The 24 topology templates are generated text ('normalized as an AIW topology-template record', brain-catalogue.js), and no code applies a TPL- id; the only reference is curation marks (catalogue-curation.js:37-38). The Chapter 7 compare-and-select mechanism (techrealisation.record, technology-realisation-domain.js:29-37, :243) has no Chapter 10 equivalent.
  - Handbook: Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › Component and architecture catalogue: CIF-S001..S012 are 'reference designs requiring local qualification'. Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF-S002 › Meaning and decision ('Choose it when…', with the alternative and its limit) and › CIF-S003 › Meaning and decision ('Backup-and-restore can cost less…'). Cloud_Infrastructure_Master › CIF Method 03 › Apply hard gates before weighted preferences. Cloud_Architecture_Handbook › CLD 05 › Apply gates before latency preferences. Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_3 › DEP 001 › Problem and selection (an alternatives table with selection condition and cost).
  - Recommendation: Add a 'Deployment approach' stage to Chapter 10. Offer at least two candidate approaches per environment set (for example single-region multi-AZ, regional active with qualified standby, partitioned multi-region, cells), drawn only from governed, released blueprint claims (knowledge source, then claim, then four-eyes review, then release). Apply hard gates deterministically (residency, recovery targets, zero-loss claims, operating capability) to exclude candidates, then show the driver-weighted lean as a reading only. Record an architect selection with rationale, using the same stale-on-change rule as a Chapter 7 selection. Plans and placements then reference the selected approach. Sol may compare candidates with citations but never select.
  - Skeptic: DOES NOT HOLD — Overstated. AIW has a generic propose, compare and select mechanism, and it works on Chapter 10 records. 'Saved design alternatives' hold up to 20 canonical commands of any type, including runtime.zone, runtime.plan and runtime.placement (workbench-domain.js:145-151). Comparing 2 to 6 of them gives the diff plus optional capacity and whole-life cost metrics (workbench-domain.js:51-58; architecture-analysis.js:18-31). Capacity is measured against failed replicas and a shared downstream limit. workspace.merge records the accepted alternative with a reason, and workspace.set-aside records 'not selected' with a reason (workbench-domain.js:166-172). Both are person-only: Sol and agent accounts are refused (authorship.js:39). The SDD prints the alternatives, their status and the comparisons in section 3 (sdd-architecture.js:693-711). Verified in node on a scratch copy of bank-payment: Alt A (a runtime.zone in 'Region B') was merged as 'accepted', and Alt B (a runtime.plan rescale) became 'not selected'. What is really missing: (a) the comparison never assesses drivers or hard constraints, so there are no gates before preferences; (b) there is no governed blueprint source: the TPL templates cannot be applied, and library entries of kind 'reference-architecture' or 'pattern' exist (workbench-domain.js:180; organization-service.js:17) but are refused for use (workbench-domain.js:74) and are listed only on Chapter 3 (workbench-ui.js:103); (c) there is no deployment-approach record that plans and placements reference. Re-label it PARTLY.
