## library-coverage

**Question.** How do the handbooks' component libraries (Enterprise_Component_Library Parts 1-3, Software_Design_Component_Dictionary_Master, Application_Architecture_Master, Cloud_Infrastructure_Master, DevOps_Master) compare with AIW's knowledge catalogue? Can AIW select components and deployment architectures that realise the architecture? Which categories, components and attributes is AIW missing, and how could the libraries be imported in a governed way (candidate, then reviewed, then released, never auto-promoted)?

**Answer.** AIW does let a person choose components and products that realise the architecture, but only partly, and never on its own. In Chapter 5, "From the library" offers project or organisation component snapshots plus 20 AKR "component archetypes", and saves any choice as a candidate that a person must review. Chapter 6 maps each need to one of 15 vendor-neutral capability categories. Chapter 7 compares product options against drivers using a 14-product catalogue with 34 mechanism traits. No chapter offers a deployment-architecture or topology catalogue.

The shipped catalogue (AKR-0.10.73.5) has 328 records. Its schema has slots for problem, context, applicability, exclusions, failure modes, obligations, alternatives, a component kit, an interface kit, quality impacts and fitness tests. But 318 of the 328 records are template text. The 20 archetypes, 30 anti-patterns and 24 topology templates are given their categories in strict rotation. As a result, proposing "Relational Data Capability" puts an OIDC/SPIFFE/JWT identity contract and a policy-decision contract into the model; I confirmed this by running the domain code in memory.

The handbooks define about 308 component definitions (ECL 120, SCD 100, AAP-C 20, CIF-C 36, DOP-C 32), plus 38 styles (12 of them cloud deployment styles), 92 patterns, 60 tactics, 60 anti-patterns, 20 compositions and 12 blueprints. Most entries carry forces, a boundary and owned state, semantic ports, physical options, typed configuration attributes, failure and repair, tests, metric contracts and evidence labels. AIW has no governed record type that can hold these, and no picker in Chapters 9 or 10 to offer them.

A governed import is feasible on AIW's existing path: source, then candidate claim, then four-eyes review, then a checksummed and signed release, then a project pin (scripts/import-candidate-set.mjs, knowledge-governance.js). It needs four additions: a "component" or "deployment-topology" architecture-unit kind with those fields, an evidence-level vocabulary, larger or tiered limits, and chapter pickers that only create reviewable candidates and never score or promote.

**Skeptic on the answer.** Three corrections to the direct answer. (1) 'AIW has no governed record type that can hold these' is overstated. The organisation-library component entry is governed: an exact source passage and licence, conditions and limitations, a different reviewer, a signed release and a project pin (organization-service.js:70-96). The snapshot check accepts any snapshot-shaped content (component-library.js:31). I ran a hand-built CL-RES-003 snapshot through it into a Chapter 5 proposal. So AIW can hold and offer a reduced profile (purpose, boundary, responsibilities, interfaces with promises, owned data, needs, obligations); what it lacks are the richer fields. (2) Proposing 'Relational Data Capability' does not put the OIDC/SPIFFE/JWT and policy-decision contracts into the model as Chapter 8 records. They are saved as `proposed` text on a candidate component whose purpose is the template sentence (realisation-domain.js:334-340). Also, the recommended stopgap, withdrawing the archetypes, has no effect in Chapter 5. The library list and realisation.candidate ignore withdrawals (component-library.js:50,68; component-library-ui.js:66,85). Only Sol retrieval honours them (architecture-brain.js:110), and I confirmed in memory that a withdrawn archetype still saves as a candidate. (3) The import path is described inaccurately. Project releases are checksummed, not signed (knowledge-governance.js:219-221); signing applies only to organisation releases. scripts/import-candidate-set.mjs fetches sources from the repository corpus through knowledge.fetch (lines 2-4, 42), and such sources need a signed repository receipt (knowledge-governance.js:58-61), so it cannot import the local handbook Markdown as it stands. Two minor points: Chapter 10 has fixed recovery-strategy and state-mode choices and a zone-loss simulation (runtime-domain.js:26-27,290-298), though no topology catalogue, so 'No chapter offers a deployment-architecture or topology catalogue' stands. Ten AKR records have alternatives, not nine.

**What AIW does today.**

- AKR pattern catalogue AKR-0.10.73.5: 328 records (14 styles, 240 patterns, 30 anti-patterns, 24 topology templates, 20 component archetypes), described as 'descriptive only' and lazy-loaded (public/catalogue-index.js:1-3; public/catalogue-store.js:1-10). Each record carries id, name, recordType, category, applicableStages, summary, problem, aliases, tags, context, prerequisites, applicabilityRules, exclusions, risks, failureModes, counterfactualExplanation, obligations[{id, title, category, description, mandatory, verificationHint}], alternatives, complements, conflicts, componentKit[{archetype, canonicalKind, responsibility, required}], interfaceKit[{contractType, protocol, interaction, providerRole, consumerRole, required}], qualityImpacts[{attributeId, direction, magnitude, conditions, rationale}], fitnessTests, knowledgeAuthority, lineageSummary, sourcePointer and recordSha256 (public/brain-catalogue.js, BRAIN_CATALOGUE.records, inventoried in-process).
- AKR governance flags: 327 records are 'descriptive-and-editorial-composition-only' with conformanceReady false, scoringCalibrated false and candidate and discovery influence 'blocked'. Only PAT-EVENT-SOURCING is 'controlled-pilot-reasoning'. 327 records have 0 direct source claims and 3 editorial-synthesis claims each (public/brain-catalogue.js knowledgeAuthority and lineageSummary). The pack is withdrawable per project (public/knowledge-governance.js:233-239). Quality-impact magnitude is deliberately dropped when the structure is read (public/knowledge-structure.js:31).
- AIW's own curation already marks 11 template records 'Not for financial use' (for example PAT-DISASTER-RECOVERY, PAT-BACKUP-AND-RESTORE, PAT-AUDIT-TRAIL and TPL-PAYMENT-PROCESSING-CELL 'no payment content'). It also holds 15 stubs for relations the catalogue names but does not contain. The curation is 'not reviewed by a person' (public/catalogue-curation.js:1-19, :28-39). Marked records are never cited as grounding (public/architecture-brain.js:110).
- SA Playbook AIW-PLAYBOOK-3: 12 attributes, 5 families, 56 tactics {attribute, group, concept, example, useCase, src, cues}, 14 patterns {effects marks, also, text, example, catalogue, src}, 5 styles and 4 anti-patterns. Each entry is exact text with a locator in hashed owner files (public/playbook-knowledge.js:1-5).
- Pattern DNA AIW-PATTERN-DNA-1: 12 'when do I…' questions. Each question opens a design question and 'never chooses an option' (public/pattern-dna.js:1-15). Trade-off pack AIW-TRADEOFFS-1: 15 passages, 'AIW-authored teaching knowledge… not calibrated', and it never affects scoring (knowledge/brain-tradeoffs.json authority).
- Technology catalogue: 14 products {id, name, category, summary, documentation} in 9 categories (public/technology-catalogue.js:10-25). It is a 'discovery projection'; the choice itself stays in technologyRealisation (:1-4). There are 34 product mechanism traits (public/product-knowledge.js:14) and 41 typed configuration fields (35 boolean, 3 enum, 3 number) for 12 products (public/product-knowledge.js:56). Chapter 6 has 15 capability categories (public/technology-domain.js:83-92).
- Chapter 5 component selection, partial: 'From the library' lists the 20 AKR archetypes and organisation component entries (public/component-library-ui.js:37,59,85). A choice builds a proposal (public/component-snapshot.js:98-121) that is saved only through the reviewed realisation.candidate path, with status 'candidate' and its origin and library reference (public/realisation-domain.js:332-341). Contracts and needs stay proposals for Chapters 8 and 6 (public/component-library.js:53-61).
- Chapter 5 pattern picker: the UI offers only the 19 Playbook styles and patterns (public/realisation-ui.js:76). The domain accepts the 75 Playbook style, pattern and tactic ids and refuses AKR ids (public/realisation-domain.js:38,240).
- Chapter 3 alternatives: `pattern` and `antiPattern` are free-text fields with datalists of the 254 AKR style and pattern names and the 30 AKR anti-patterns. Chapter 2 `tactic` is free text with the 56 Playbook tactics (public/record-fields.js:126,131,427-436). Playbook styles are mapped to AKR styles in public/design-reasoning.js:460-469.
- Chapters 9 and 10 have no pattern, tactic or topology picker (public/record-fields.js:180-183,190-192). The AKR security and deployment patterns and the 24 topology templates reach those chapters only through Sol retrieval and the knowledge workspace (public/architecture-brain.js:110,126). No chapter code reads topology templates; the only references outside the catalogue are curation marks (public/catalogue-curation.js:37-38,53).
- Chapter 11 detects 8 catalogue anti-patterns with hard-coded rules (SPOF, synchronous chain, missing idempotency, retry storm, shared DB, god service, unbounded queue, observability afterthought) (public/design-spec.js:246-281).
- Project library (workspace.catalogue) kinds: technology, control, runbook, requirement, scenario, decision and document templates, standard, pattern, reference-architecture and component. Status is draft, approved or deprecated; approving needs a project source, a review note and reviewed=true (public/workbench-domain.js:179-187). Approved technology, control and runbook entries can seed a Chapter 7 option, a Chapter 9 control or a Chapter 10 runbook. Pattern, standard and reference-architecture entries cannot be applied to any record (public/workbench-domain.js:68-74).
- A component library entry must be a snapshot (aiw.component-snapshot.v1) filed from an existing project component; anything else is rejected (public/component-library.js:28-31). An adopted entry records releaseId and recordSha256, or entry revision and contentHash (public/component-library.js:63-70).
- Organisation library: entries carry an exact source passage, revision, licence hash, conditions, limitations, lifecycle and reviewDue (organization-service.js:69-76). A different authenticated reviewer must review each entry (organization-service.js:78-81). Administrators publish signed releases (organization-service.js:85-87). Adoption installs an 'approved' project entry (organization-service.js:90-97). It runs locally through worker.js (server.js:11).
- Governed claims: knowledge.source (at most 60,000 characters, SHA-256) then knowledge.claim (passages of at most 101 lines and 12,000 characters, with conditions and limitations) then four-eyes review then a checksummed release then activate and link (public/knowledge-governance.js:132-135,164-172,218). Eligibility is re-checked on every read, including that the author is not the verifier (public/knowledge-governance.js:41-66). Limits are 100 sources, 250 claims and 30 releases per project; an organisation release allows 1,000 claims, 300 sources and 6 MB (public/knowledge-governance.js:32,165,185,218).
- Structured claim 'architectureUnit' kinds: style, pattern, tactic, anti-pattern-signal, product-mechanism, design-principle and quality-tradeoff. Fields: mechanism, scope {capability, product, version, topology, tier, operatingModel}, effects, obligations {action, evidence}, exclusions, typed applicability predicates and placement (public/architecture-knowledge-unit.js:6-17,51-80). When read, a reviewed claim gets empty componentRoles, interfaceRoles, verificationHints and failureModes (public/knowledge-structure.js:46-48).
- Candidate-set import already exists. It creates candidate claims, authored by the importer, through the project's own commands; a different person must verify each one, and nothing is released or activated (scripts/import-candidate-set.mjs:1-6; candidate-sets/*.json authority). The drafter never reads candidate or discovery knowledge (architecture-drafter.js:10-11). Review, release, activate and withdraw are person-only, and Sol cannot claim an 'approved' state (authorship.js:39-45).

**Gaps.**

- **[critical | 5 (also 6, 7, 8, 10); X4 knowledge | effort L] No governed record type can hold a handbook component profile, so AIW cannot offer the libraries' components as reviewed, selectable building blocks**
  - AIW: CANNOT. Three places look like they could hold a profile, and none can. (1) AKR records are a shipped, read-only projection; AIW 'never edits it' (public/catalogue-curation.js:3-5). (2) A component entry in the project or organisation library must be a snapshot filed from an existing project component (public/component-library.js:28-31; organization-service.js:73). (3) Governed claims have no 'component' or 'deployment-topology' kind (public/architecture-knowledge-unit.js:6), and when read their componentRoles, interfaceRoles, verificationHints and failureModes are empty (public/knowledge-structure.js:46-48). The Chapter 5 component record has no field for a pinned profile version or for semantic ports and configuration attributes, beyond `library` {releaseId, recordSha256} (public/component-library.js:63-70).
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Govern evidence versions and changes › Define a component record that can evolve: a governed profile has 'a stable identity, a version, its semantic kind, applicability, attributes, ports, allowed relationships, constraints, alternatives, operating responsibilities and source evidence', and a profile update creates 'a reviewable migration proposal'. ECL Part 1 › Reusable decision and evidence templates › Project component instance (TPL-COMP) and › Common model attributes (profile_id, profile_version, lifecycle Proposed/reviewed/adopted/active/retiring/retired, evidence_ids). ECL Part 1 › Build useful architecture views from one model › Apply a component in a workbench: dragging a library type 'creates a proposed project object… with a reference to the profile version', and the architect can 'accept, amend or reject'.
  - Recommendation: Add 'component', 'composition' and 'deployment-topology' kinds to architectureUnit. Give them typed fields: boundary, ownedState, invariants, ports[{name, direction, contract}], physicalOptions[{title, topology, configuration, operatingCost, products}], configuration[{name, type, unit, example, required, validation, rationale}], failureModes[{failure, behaviour, recovery, evidence}], tests[{id, given, when, then, requiredEvidence, evidenceLevel}], metricContract (the 10 handbook fields), operations{ownerRole, signals, runbook, security, evolution} and relationships[{verb, targetId, reason}]. Populate these in reviewedRecord. Let realisation.candidate take a released profile claim as its origin, pinning profile id, version and claim receipt. Keep authority descriptive: scoring false, no hard constraint or conformance unless a person separately adopts it as policy.
  - Skeptic: DOES NOT HOLD — The gap is overstated: AIW does this PARTLY. It is not true that AIW CANNOT. The organisation-library 'component' entry is a governed record type. It carries an exact source passage, a licence, conditions, limitations and a review date (organization-service.js:70-75). A different authenticated reviewer must review it (organization-service.js:80), it is published in a signed release (organization-service.js:85-87) and a project pins it (organization-service.js:89-96). Chapter 5 'From the library' then offers it as a reviewable candidate (component-library-ui.js:26-33,66; realisation-domain.js:337-341). The analyst says an entry 'must be a snapshot filed from an existing project component', but readComponentSnapshot checks only the schema id and identity.title (component-library.js:31). I built a 'Circuit breaker (CL-RES-003)' snapshot by hand with no origin project and ran it through readComponentSnapshot and libraryProposal in memory. It produced a valid Chapter 5 proposal with its boundary, a provided contract with promises, a need and an obligation. The UI files entries only from a saved component (component-library-ui.js:41-49), but the API accepts any snapshot-shaped content (organization-service.js:70,73). So a reduced profile fits today: purpose, boundary, responsibilities, provides/requires interfaces with promises, owned/consumed/produced data, technology needs and obligations, within 30,000 characters and 24 items per list (component-library.js:19-21,32-43). What is missing is the profile richness: forces, alternatives, physical options, typed configuration, failure and repair, tests, a metric contract, evidence level and semantic kind. That overlaps the attributes gap. Two remedies are possible: new architectureUnit kinds, or extending this existing four-eyes, signed snapshot schema.
- **[high | 5 (feeds 6 and 8) | effort S] The 20 shipped component archetypes are template text with wrong category kits, and proposing one injects wrong contracts and needs into Chapter 5**
  - AIW: PARTLY works, but misleads. Of the 328 AKR records, 318 have the template summary '…normalized as an AIW <kind> record…' (public/brain-catalogue.js, inventoried in-process). Only STYLE-MICROSERVICES and 9 patterns are hand-written. Archetype, anti-pattern and topology-template categories rotate in strict order: ARCH-RELATIONAL-DATA-CAPABILITY and ARCH-CACHE-CAPABILITY are 'security', ARCH-POLICY-ENGINE-CAPABILITY is 'observability', ANTI-SINGLE-POINT-OF-FAILURE is 'security', TPL-CELL-BASED-WORKLOAD is 'ai'. Component kits are 11 per-category templates. I ran archetypeProposal plus applyRealisationCommand in memory: 'Relational Data Capability' is saved as candidate kind 'store' with purpose '…normalized as an AIW component-archetype record…', contracts 'Identity Assertion [contract OIDC/SPIFFE/JWT]' and 'Policy Decision', and security needs 'Policy Enforcement Point' and 'Trust Service' (public/component-snapshot.js:98-121; public/realisation-domain.js:332-341). The Chapter 5 library offers all 20 (public/component-library-ui.js:85). The curation marks 11 records but no archetypes (public/catalogue-curation.js:28-39).
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_2 › DAT 001 Relational database, and Software_Design_Component_Dictionary_Master › SCD-C040 Relational data store, define relational stores by transaction boundary, isolation and ownership, not by identity assertions. ECL Part 1 › Choose technologies using the required semantics › Compare capabilities before products: 'The label relational is insufficient to prove that a particular schema and isolation policy protect a balance.'
  - Recommendation: Until replacements are released, a project steward should withdraw the 20 archetypes with the existing knowledge.withdraw (public/knowledge-governance.js:233-239), or add curation marks that hide them from the Chapter 5 library. Report the rotated categories and template content back to the AKR maintainer. Replace them through the governed import (see the import gap), with ECL and SCD profiles as candidates. Never patch the hashed catalogue in place.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Confirmed in memory. ARCH-RELATIONAL-DATA-CAPABILITY has category 'security' and the generic security kit. archetypeProposal gives kind 'store', the template purpose, the 'Identity Assertion [contract OIDC/SPIFFE/JWT]' and 'Policy Decision' contracts, and the Policy Enforcement Point and Trust Service needs (component-snapshot.js:98-121). The categories rotate on a 5-step cycle for archetypes and anti-patterns and a 6-step cycle for topology templates. Two corrections. (1) The contracts and needs are stored as the `proposed` text on the candidate (realisation-domain.js:334-340). They do not become Chapter 8 or Chapter 6 records. (2) The recommended remedy does not work. knowledge.withdraw accepts AKR record ids (knowledge-governance.js:234), but only Sol retrieval honours a withdrawal (architecture-brain.js:110). The Chapter 5 library list uses the static COMPONENT_ARCHETYPES (component-library.js:50; component-library-ui.js:66,85; design-resources-ui.js:131). realisation.candidate checks only isArchetypeId (component-library.js:68; realisation-domain.js:337-340). I withdrew ARCH-RELATIONAL-DATA-CAPABILITY in memory, then saved a realisation.candidate from it, and it still saved as a 'store' with the OIDC/SPIFFE/JWT contract. Curation marks do not hide records either: a marked record is 'shown with its mark' (catalogue-curation.js:9-10). A steward therefore has no way to remove the archetypes from Chapter 5; it needs an engineering change.
- **[high | 5, 6, 7, 10 | effort XL] Whole component categories in the handbooks have no AIW archetype or product entry**
  - AIW: PARTLY: there are 20 archetypes (public/component-library.js:50) and 14 products in 9 of the 15 capability categories (public/technology-catalogue.js:10-25; public/technology-domain.js:83-92). Missing as component entries: business components (ledger, payment orchestrator, reconciliation engine; AKR PAT-RECONCILIATION-LOOP is marked a 'platform control loop', public/catalogue-curation.js:36), execution units, network and traffic constructs, deployment and failure-domain constructs, protection controls (DLP, consent, malware scanning, retention and deletion), observability stores, delivery-chain components and endpoint types. AKR has only template patterns for some of these (for example AVAILABILITY-ZONE-DISTRIBUTION, CONTAINERIZATION, WEB-APPLICATION-FIREWALL, TOKENIZATION). It has a stub for DUAL-WRITE with no content (public/catalogue-curation.js:9-14).
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library Parts 1-3 have 120 profiles in 15 families of 8: APP, INT, MSG, BUS, RES (Part 1); DAT, DST, AIN, SEC, PRO (Part 2); RUN, NET, DEP, OBS, DEL (Part 3). By header kind: 49 technology capability, 34 application responsibility, 18 policy mechanism, 12 deployment construct, 7 delivery capability. They are listed under the family headings, for example ECL Part 3 › Deployment and failure domains, › Connectivity and traffic, and ECL Part 1 › Enterprise business components. ECL Part 1 › Compose neighboring component roles adds 20 compositions (CL-VAR: CDN, persistent job scheduler, ESB, database access proxy…). Software_Design_Component_Dictionary_Master › Component and architecture catalogue lists 100 (Messaging and integration 20, Business behavior 20, State and storage 12, Runtime and evidence 10, Identity and authority 10, Channels 10, Capacity and resilience 10, Delivery and recovery 8). Cloud_Infrastructure_Master CIF-C001..036, DevOps_Master DOP-C001..032 and Application_Architecture_Master AAP-C001..020 add 88 more.
  - Recommendation: Import in tranches by family. Map each ECL semantic kind to the AIW chapter that takes it up: application responsibility to Chapter 4/5, technology capability to Chapter 6 category templates, policy mechanism to Chapter 5/9, deployment construct to Chapter 10, delivery capability to Chapter 10/11. Add Chapter 6 category templates for the families the catalogue lacks. ## traceability

**Question.** Can a person follow one requirement end to end in AIW (requirement → architectural driver/quality scenario → decision → logical responsibility → application component → technology capability → technology product → interface/data contract → security control → deployment placement → review finding), forwards and backwards, in the Model views and in the SDD? Where does the chain break or depend on free text, and what does AIW lack compared with the handbooks' traceability guidance? Walked live on the clinic-appointments reference (REQ-005, offer a freed slot within 15 minutes) and the bank-payment reference (REQ-003, prevent duplicate financial effects).

**Answer.** Only partly. AIW keeps one canonical graph and has a forward requirement trail: the Chapter 11 trail walk, the review-desk Trace, the traceability-matrix annex and the narrative Story. On both reference projects (clinic-appointments REQ-005, bank-payment REQ-003) each hop from requirement to runtime plan shows something. But the trail is a set of records per layer, not a recorded chain. Responsibilities are found only by a direct requirement link. Contracts, data and controls are pulled in because they touch the same component. Threats appear only if someone names the requirement on the threat. The "product" is the realisation plan, and the desk shows the first unselected candidate as if it were the product. Review findings and verification evidence never appear. Five separate traversals give different answers for the same requirement. For clinic REQ-005 the controls are SEC-004 and SEC-005 in the trail, SEC-005 in the narrative, and none in the component specification and SDD §4. Going backwards there are only one-hop backlinks and a 4-hop Matrix chain that ties every bank requirement to every threat. A person can also record a trace review on a broken trail, and that clears the trace warning. Compared with the handbooks' two-way, typed chain that ends in evidence, AIW lacks typed links for each hop, hops for threats, products, verification and findings, a reverse trail, budgets per hop, and an SDD section that prints the chain. Probe data and outputs are in /home/user/hb-review/scratch/trace-e2e/ (scratch database only; the AIW repository was not changed).

**Skeptic on the answer.** The overall verdict, 'only partly', and most of the live data hold. I re-ran the trail, desk and narrative on the scratch documents and got the same results. The handbook quotes check out except one, which sits under a different heading (see the free-text gap). Five statements are wrong or overstated. (1) 'Going backwards there are only one-hop backlinks and a 4-hop Matrix chain that ties every bank requirement to every threat' is wrong. traceFrom gives a layered backward trace from any record (component, threat, zone, TR plan, placement) in the Model trace path and the Story (model-matrix.js:168-194; notation-view.js:1815-1825; story-view.js:511). It keeps the shortest chain, so THR-002 reaches only REQ-003. The real weakness is that it rests on untyped Matrix chains: from SEC-002 it finds '[no requirement]'. (2) 'Five separate traversals': the narrative and the Matrix are one algorithm (model-matrix.js:160-164), and the architecture case filters reviewTrace (architecture-case.js:31). Other traversals exist and also disagree: driver-scope.js, driverChain → ARS (design-reasoning.js:130; ars-document.js:84-87), and the SDD journey line (sdd-architecture.js:1236-1251). The ARS says 'Controls: none' for QD-005/REQ-005. (3) 'Review findings and verification evidence never appear' is overstated. The trail walk's inspector lists source findings for each selected record (review-ui.js:149-152). The end-to-end architecture case collects typed measurements, control verification and runtime exercises for one requirement spine (architecture-case.js:52, :151, :163). They do not appear on the trail, desk, annex or SDD. (4) 'AIW lacks … a reverse trail, budgets per hop' is wrong on both counts. A reverse trace exists (point 1). The desk computes a latency budget per synchronous level with a timeout nesting rule (desk-vitals.js:151-170), and Chapter 6, 7 and 10 targets name their driver and are checked for agreement (driver-links.js). What is missing is a typed reverse trail and a budget a person allocates, shown on the trail. (5) 'Threats appear only if someone names the requirement on the threat' is true of the forward trail, but the Model trace from a threat reaches its requirement. The rest is confirmed: the trail is built layer by layer, the desk shows an unselected product as the product, review.trace is accepted on a broken trail and clears the warning, and the SDD body prints no chain. Probe scripts are in /home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-trace/; nothing in /home/user/AIW-Brain was changed.

**What AIW does today.**

- Chapter 11 requirement trail (reviewTrace) joins one requirement to drivers, decisions, responsibilities, components, needs, capabilities, realisations, contracts, data, controls, runtime plans, parties, threats, paths and zones, shown as five walk steps (public/review-domain.js:153-178). Drivers and responsibilities come from a direct requirement or group link (:158, :163). Decisions also come by shared driver (:162). Components come via Chapter 4 mappings; capabilities via needs and their TM mappings; realisations via RM mappings. Contracts, controls and runtime plans come by inherited sources (:129-131, :171-175). Threats, parties and paths come only by a direct link (:176).
- Trail walk in the Chapter 11 Model tab: the canvas is filtered to the trail's nodes, five steps with play/pause, and 'Record trace review' only after all five are visited (public/review-ui.js:145, :157-163). review.trace needs a reviewer and notes and keeps a fingerprint per trail, so an edit on the trail reopens it (public/review-domain.js:226-229, :418). There is a per-requirement 'trace' warning and milestone 3 (:282, :299).
- Review-desk Trace table: one row per requirement with chips per chapter, naming the first broken link on the spine (responsibilities, components, capabilities, products, runtime) (public/desk-model.js:21-31, :71-100). Every row ends 'Design links do not verify implementation' (:101-103).
- Traceability-matrix annex (CSV/JSON): every requirement against 10 layers, separating records that name it from records reached through the design (public/sdd-annexes.js:69-88).
- Model Matrix: any two record types, as direct, one intermediate record, or a shortest chain up to 4 hops, in both directions, 60×40 per screen (public/model-matrix.js:26-38, :52-102).
- Narrative trace (notation/Story views): requirement → driver → decision → responsibility → component → capability → realisation → contract/data → control → placement → running part. It names gaps honestly, e.g. 'No control is recorded for this step yet', 'TR-001 lists 2 options; none is selected yet' (public/trace-narrative.js:1-12; public/model-matrix.js:165-185; used at public/notation-view.js:1263).
- Canonical graph edges exist for most hops: requirement links ('fulfils'), driver→responsibility ('constrains'), decision alternatives ('considers', with a selected flag), responsibility→component ('realizedBy'), component→need→capability, capability→realisation→option ('considers', with a selected flag), contract provides/uses/exchanges, control protects/mitigates, threat threatens, plan operatedAs/enforces, placement placedAs/locatedIn (public/architecture-model.js:146-213).
- The architecture requirements specification (ARS) reports each quality driver as measurable, verified and carried, and names drivers that no capability, realisation or runtime plan carries. Clinic QD-005 is reported as 'named by no runtime plan, capability or realisation' (public/ars-document.js:74-95, :160-176).
- End-to-end architecture case: nine checks along one requirement spine. The spine runs driver, decision, responsibility, mapped component, allocated capability, realisation, runtime asset. The checks add a confirmed target, two compared approaches, a recorded rationale, two compared products, a specified selection, placement with a failure domain, and a current test (public/architecture-case.js:124-165).
- Component specification and SDD §4 per component: responsibilities, requirements, technologies (capability → product marked 'Candidate option'), interfaces, data, protection and placement (public/component-spec-document.js:72; public/sdd-architecture.js:985-1032).
- Change impact: a changed requirement opens reviews on every record its trail reaches, grouped by chapter (shared/projects-domain.js:160-180). Downstream fingerprints raise source-change.
- One-hop backlinks: the 'Object & backlinks' page lists saved relationships in both directions (public/object-exploration.js:17, :19).
- A quality driver must link a responsibility (the 'responsibilities' error, public/quality-domain.js:180). A 'cited-not-linked' warning fires when a record cites a requirement id in its words without linking it (public/requirement-links.js:107).
- Through Sol only: the 'What changes if…' preset traces recorded connections and names gaps, as a proposal only (public/sol-question-presets.js:8). Sol and agent accounts cannot send review.trace (authorship.js:39, :57-63).

**Gaps.**

- **[critical | Ch 11 Review & realize (trail), cross-cutting Ch 2-10 | effort L] The trail is a set of records per layer, not a recorded causal chain, so it both omits and over-includes records**
  - AIW: PARTLY. reviewTrace builds each layer on its own (public/review-domain.js:158-176). Responsibilities come only from a direct requirement or group link (:163), never from driver.responsibilityIds or a chosen alternative's responsibilityIds. Alternatives' responsibilityIds and componentIds are not graph edges at all (public/architecture-model.js:170-172). Contracts, data and controls are inherited from any component they touch (public/security-domain.js:43; review-domain.js:171-174). Bank REQ-003, live: QD-001 names responsibilities 'api' and 'ledger', but the trail holds only LR-003. IF-001 Payment initiation, where same-reference retries arrive, is missing. SEC-002 Audit trail, which mitigates THR-004 and not duplicates, is listed as REQ-003's control. Clinic REQ-005, live: the trail includes IF-004 'Hand over reminder', DAT-004 Reminder and SEC-004, only because they touch APP-005.
  - Handbook: Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Preserve the decision chain: 'A realization should link back to the responsibility it implements, while evidence should link to the specific obligation it verifies.' Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Derive component responsibilities from drivers: 'The chain from requirement to component can be inspected.' Architecture_Assurance_Production_Readiness_and_Evolution › Trace a complete claim, not a matching filename: 'A file that contains the word authorization does not establish that every consequential operation checks current authority.'
  - Recommendation: Give each hop a typed link that says which requirement or quality scenario it serves: driver→responsibility, chosen alternative→responsibility/component, contract operation→requirement, control→threat for that requirement. Compute the trail as paths over those links, and show for each record the path that reached it. Label records found only because they touch the same component as 'context', not as carriers.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds for the Chapter 11 trail. I reproduced it on the scratch documents: bank REQ-003 gives only LR-003 although QD-001.responsibilityIds is ['ledger','api']. IF-001 is missing, and SEC-002 (threatIds ['thr-004']) is included. Clinic REQ-005 picks up IF-004, DAT-004 and SEC-004 only because they touch APP-005 (review-domain.js:161-176, security-domain.js:43). Three refinements. (1) AIW does read driver.responsibilityIds and the chosen alternative's responsibilityIds elsewhere: driver-scope.js:31-49 builds typed driver paths (direct-responsibility, shared-requirement, decision, responsibility-mapping), and logical-domain.js:57-70 uses both for a responsibility's drivers and decisions. So reviewTrace disagrees with AIW's own Chapter 4 reading; the information is not simply absent. (2) A typed requirement link already exists on contracts, controls, threats, data, plans, needs, capabilities and TR records (requirementIds/requirementGroupIds, architecture-model.js:148, :213-215), and the trail adds what is named (review-domain.js:154-158). The missing parts are that drafting leaves these links empty and that driver and alternative links are not used. (3) The recommendation to label records reached only by touching the same component is already done in the matrix annex, which splits 'named' from 'through the design' (sdd-annexes.js:75-79). The trail walk, desk and narrative do not make that split.
- **[critical | Cross-cutting (Model, Ch 5 spec, Ch 11 desk/annex, SDD) | effort M] Five separate trace algorithms give different answers, so the Model views and the SDD disagree**
  - AIW: CANNOT give one answer. The traversals are: reviewTrace (review-domain.js:153; used by the trail, desk, annex and change impact), traceFrom/narrative (model-matrix.js:165-185), the Matrix chain (model-matrix.js:76-96), the architecture-case spine (architecture-case.js:128-149), and the design index behind the component spec and SDD §4, which counts only controls and threats aimed directly at the component (design-spec.js:131-132, :165; sdd-architecture.js:1023-1025). Live, clinic REQ-005 controls: SEC-004 and SEC-005 in the trail, SEC-005 in the narrative, 'Controls: none · Threats: none' in the APP-005 specification, and no Protection line in SDD §4. Bank REQ-003: SEC-002 in the trail, 'No control is recorded for this step yet' in the narrative, 'Controls: none · Threats: none' in the APP-003 specification. The narrative includes TC-002 Transactional persistence; the trail does not.
  - Handbook: Architecture_Assurance_Production_Readiness_and_Evolution › AS-P12 Keep one model lineage and separate approval: 'Generate compatible SA, SD and register views from the same bound facts.' Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Use views to explain one architecture: 'Views are projections of these relationships, not independent drawings that must be manually reconciled after each change.'
  - Recommendation: Build one trace service. The trail, desk, matrix annex, narrative, component specification, SDD §4 and change impact should all read from it. Add a regression test that asserts every output gives the same per-hop sets for the reference projects.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The conclusion holds; the count is wrong. The narrative and the Matrix chain are one algorithm: traceFrom reads each step from matrixModel (model-matrix.js:160-164, :176). The architecture-case spine is a filter over reviewTrace, not a separate traversal (architecture-case.js:31, :129-136). There are other traversals the analyst did not count. driver-scope.js:16-73 is used by the component specification and design questions. driverChain (design-reasoning.js:130) is used by the ARS. The SDD journey line reads only logical.connections (sdd-architecture.js:1236-1251). They also disagree. The ARS prints 'Controls: none' for clinic QD-005/REQ-005, which is a fourth answer (ars-document.js:86-87; clinic.ars.md line 134). Tracing backwards in the Model from bank SEC-002 gives '[no requirement]', yet reviewTrace lists SEC-002 as REQ-003's control. The verified figures stand: trail SEC-004/SEC-005, narrative SEC-005, spec 'Controls: none', and narrative TC-002 not in the trail.
- **[high | Ch 9 Security / Ch 11 | effort M] Threats are missing from the trail, the matrix annex, the narrative and change impact**
  - AIW: CANNOT, unless a person names the requirement on the threat. reviewTrace takes threats only by direct link (review-domain.js:176). TRACE_ORDER has no threat step (model-matrix.js:165). MATRIX_LAYERS has no threats (sdd-annexes.js:69). requirementImpact never opens a review on a threat (shared/projects-domain.js:174). Drafted threats name no requirements. Live: bank THR-002 'Repeated instruction changes the financial effect' raises Chapter 9 'uncovered' (E) and has no mitigating control, yet it is absent from REQ-003's trail, its narrative and its desk row. Clinic THR-005 targets DAT-005, which APP-005 owns, and is likewise absent.
  - Handbook: Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Translate business promises into measurable requirements: 'Include failure, peak, recovery, security and change scenarios.' Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Use obligations as release gates: 'No duplicate financial effect | Same-intent retry after every crash boundary | Provider effect count and balanced local journals.'

TRACE_ORDER has no threat step (model-matrix.js:165), MATRIX_LAYERS has no threat layer (sdd-annexes.js:69), and requirementImpact uses only t.controls for Chapter 9 (shared/projects-domain.js:175). Bank THR-002 raises the error 'uncovered', and every drafted threat has requirementIds empty. Two refinements. A person can link a threat to a requirement (requirementIds on threats, architecture-model.js:204), and it then joins the trail. The Model trace path also works backwards from a threat: traceFrom(THR-002) gives 'THR-002 → REQ-003 → QD-001…' (model-matrix.js:171; notation-view.js:1815-1825). So a threat can be traced back to its requirement, but it never shows on that requirement's forward thread.

Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Preserve the decision chain: 'When a product, region or schema changes, traverse these links to find affected tests and owners.'

Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Produce an architecture dossier another team can continue: 'An accepted requirement with no verification is an evidence gap.'

And AIW already defines conditions for each hop, as the recommendation asks, in the end-to-end architecture case: decision compared and recorded, product compared and selected with a specification, placement in a zone with a failure domain, and a current test (architecture-case.js:154-164). Those checks run only inside an architecture case that an architect opens; they are not used by the desk Trace or by describeTrace.

QD-005's measurement reads 'Verify against the acceptance criteria of REQ-005, once they say what is observed'. Its window is an open question. Enterprise_Component_Library_Part_1 › Use typed relationships: 'verifies | Evidence to obligation.' Quality_Architecture_Volume_9 › QAE-CMP-06 Validation evidence and permission to proceed.

Guided-task measurements carry requirementId, driverId, metric, mean, unit, samples, environment, workload, source and reviewer (architecture-case.js:52). No view answers 'which requirements and scenarios depend on ZON-x, the TR-x selection, THR-x or this finding', or which components have no driver.

Quality_Architecture_Volume_9 › QAE-CMP-01 Outcome, quality attribute, measure and mechanism: 'Write the chain in both directions.'

The residual gap is that the backward trace inherits the Matrix's untyped shortest chains, which can be wrong (SEC-002 → '[no requirement]'), and that findings cannot be traced.

The chosen alternative's responsibilityIds are read as a decision→responsibility link in Chapter 4 (logical-domain.js:67-70) and kept by decision.apply-impact (decisions-domain.js:227-228). design-questions.js:1-6 also reads which decision, selection, contract policy, control or plan answers each design question a driver opens.
- **[medium | Ch 2 Drivers → Ch 6-10 | effort L] A quality target is not allocated along the chain (no budget per hop)**
  - AIW: PARTLY. TR and runtime-plan target rows can name targetDriver, and the desk vitals compare timeouts with response targets. Live: clinic QD-005 (at most 15 minutes) reaches no capability (TC-005 driverIds []), no TR target and no RUN target. The ARS reports 'QD-005 is named by no runtime plan, capability or realisation' (ars-document.js:95). The drafted technology need is generic 'compute to run what it owns' and does not say what serves the 15-minute promise. Contracts have no timeout budget.
  - Handbook: Quality_Architecture_Volume_9 › QAE-OS-004 Quality budgets across a business journey: 'Budgets translate an overall promise into local responsibilities…' Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Derive component responsibilities from drivers: 'A quality driver of bounded confirmation latency may lead to a deadline budget, a timeout hierarchy…'
  - Recommendation: Let a driver's target be allocated to hops (contract timeout budget, capability service level, plan target row) with a stated combination rule. Show the allocated total against the target on the trail.

The desk computes a latency budget for each hop: a performance driver's target is divided over the levels of its synchronous timeout path ('≈ N ms a level'). Each contract's timeoutMs must sit inside its caller's, and an unconfirmed timeout is marked (desk-vitals.js:151-170). Contracts therefore do carry timeout figures checked against the budget. Carriers also state targets for each hop: a Chapter 6 capability's recovery target names its driver, and Chapter 7 and Chapter 10 target rows name a driver with operator, value, unit and window. Each is checked against the driver's target and marked 'breaks' when it disagrees (driver-links.js:1-12, :18-21; ars-document.js:88, :96, :173). The narrower gap is real. No person records the allocation, so the split is an equal share and only latency is combined. Nothing covers an asynchronous promise such as clinic QD-005 (15 minutes), and no budget is shown on the requirement trail. The ARS already flags QD-005 as named by no carrier (ars-document.js:95).

Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10 › Use views to explain one architecture: 'Retain identity across views.'

It has no capability, product, contract or placement, and it gives yet another control answer for QD-005 ('none').

- **[low | Ch 8 / Model Matrix | effort S] Drafted contracts follow journey order, and the Matrix reads 'fulfils' backwards**
  - AIW: PARTLY. The journey drafter creates a 'Hand over' contract between the components of consecutive journey steps (public/journey-drafter.js:400-403). It records the requirement as provenance, not as requirementIds. So clinic REQ-005 inherits 'Hand over reminder', and IF-005 runs from Freed slot handling to Missed-appointment record keeping. The Matrix explains a chain as 'REQ-005 fulfils QD-006', with the relationship's meaning reversed (architecture-model.js:148; model-matrix.js:34).

The requirement goes only into provenance.journey.upstream and requirementIds stays empty; clinic IF-005 runs obj-005 → obj-006. The 'fulfils' edge runs requirement → record (architecture-model.js:148). The canonical label is 'fulfilled by' (architecture-model.js:138), but the Matrix's WORD table reads it from the 'from' side as 'fulfils' (model-matrix.js:34-35), so the meaning is reversed. Small point: the drafted contract's provenance already records that journey rules drafted it (mode 'deterministic'), so 'mark contracts inferred from journey order' partly exists, as provenance only.

## deliverables-and-views

**Question.** Do AIW's outputs and views give the architect the deliverables the handbooks prescribe (SDD and its sections, ADRs, the view set, quality/NFR register, risk/issue register, assumptions and constraints, interface and data specifications, security architecture and threat model, deployment and environment design, operations/runbooks, transition/roadmap, operational acceptance and handover pack)? And does each output trace back to the records that justify it?

**Answer.** Partly. AIW assembles a large set of deliverables by rule. For the bank-payment reference project, the SDD exported in 2 editions × 3 audiences × 5 formats, plus the Mermaid and no-figure variants, and every one of those 36 exports returned HTTP 200. The other deliverables also came out cleanly: MADR decision records, an Architecture Requirements Specification (ARS, the quality/NFR register), component specifications, OpenAPI/AsyncAPI/JSON Schema files, ten chapter registers, a traceability matrix and twelve section-5 figures (C4 levels 1 to 3, deployment and eight chapter models). Every statement names its record IDs and its provenance. Several deliverables are only partial, because their content is free-text fields or sits only in annexes and registers rather than in the SDD body: the security architecture and threat model, the deployment design, runbooks, the transition plan, quality measurement contracts and the ADR lifecycle (review triggers, validation plans, a superseded-by link). The default architecture edition, which is the one a baseline freezes, names no threat at all (the executive edition lists THR-001 to THR-004, two of them with no control), and it has no risk treatment, delivery-obligation or assurance part; the narrative's approach field is printed in no SDD edition. AIW cannot produce a combined risks/assumptions/issues/dependencies (RAID) register, a glossary, an operational acceptance or launch handover pack, a table mapping stakeholder concerns to views (ISO 42010), or exported data, lineage, lifecycle and cooperation views (they exist only on screen). Traceability is strong: stable IDs, a provenance appendix, SHA-256 hashes on every figure and a matrix that separates named links from links derived through the design. It is weakened because the SDD body calls a driver QD1 while the matrix calls it QD-001, the IDs in the HTML are not links, and the MADR files print old priority labels. Sol only proposes framing and field text: it does not assemble any deliverable and is blocked from recording reviews, baselines or approvals.

**Skeptic on the answer.** Four parts of the direct answer are wrong or overstated. (1) 'The default architecture edition, which is the one a baseline freezes, names no threat at all' is true only for figures=none. The default export is figures=svg (worker.js:351), chapter models are on by default (public/sdd-document.js:152, :197-204), and a freeze keeps the figures (:449). So the default and frozen edition both carry §5 Figure 11 'Chapter 9 · Security & trust', which names THR-001 and THR-004 and reads '4 threats, 2 controls' (sdd_architecture_all.md:1000-1038). What is true is that no part of the text body is a threat model or risk-treatment section. (2) 'AIW cannot produce exported data, lineage, lifecycle and cooperation views (they exist only on screen)' contradicts the analyst's own capability item. Those scenes (public/notation-model.js:49-61) download from the Model tab as SVG, PNG, Structurizr, C4-PlantUML, Mermaid, draw.io and ArchiMate (public/notation-view.js:1965-1975). They are only missing from the assembled SDD, figures annex and register HTML. (3) The security finding misses that AIW already has threat elicitation: it flags every trust-boundary crossing that has no threat (public/threat-model.js:235-245) and drafts threats by rule for each external contract, confidential data definition and zone (public/journey-drafter.js:419-430; public/security-domain.js:189-195). Also, artifact/configuration identity is a field (runtime.plan releaseReference, public/record-fields.js:192), the browser PDF does draw figures (public/review-documents.js:90-105), and §3 prints capacity and cost when saved comparisons exist (public/sdd-architecture.js:693-711). (4) The traceability verdict is too generous, because a real gap is missed. The traceability matrix (public/sdd-annexes.js:69, MATRIX_LAYERS) and the requirement traceability register (annex_registers.md:71-95) stop at controls and runtime plans. They have no threat, risk-treatment, delivery-action, verification-evidence or plateau layer, so no output traces a requirement through to its verification evidence (Software_Application_Design_Handbook › Work a traceability slice). The ID mismatch is also inside a single document, not just between outputs: the SVG figures use QD-00n while the SDD's own text uses QDn. The rest checks out: 30 edition × audience × format exports plus 2 Mermaid and 4 no-figure exports make 36; approach is never printed; the frozen edition has no assurance or obligations part; Sol is barred from review commands (authorship.js:37-60). The handbook citations I checked exist and say what is claimed.

**What AIW does today.**

- SDD editions and formats — PRODUCES. The architecture and executive editions, each for the audiences all, delivery and review, in Markdown, HTML, JSON, Word and PDF; figures as SVG, none, or Mermaid (Markdown only) (worker.js:349-357, :361, :371-416; public/sdd-document.js:48-53). Runtime: all 36 exports for bank-payment returned HTTP 200 (/home/user/hb-review/scratch/outputs-views/out/sdd_*.{md,html,json,docx,pdf}). Architecture/all is 407 KB of Markdown with inline SVG, 48 KB without figures. edition=full falls back to architecture (sdd_full_legacy.md is the same size, 407,424 bytes). Word embeds the SVG figures (14 word/media entries). The server PDF draws no figure; it prints 'not drawn in this PDF' (worker.js:352; seen in sdd_architecture_all.pdf).
- SDD architecture edition structure — PRODUCES (fixed headings). Document control (version, status, owner, audience, baseline; 'Approvals: none recorded'; content readiness), 1 Introduction (purpose, key functionalities, target users, business problems, personas, scope), 2 Drivers by kind with a summary table, 3 Decisions (decision log, per-decision entries, trade-off arithmetic), 4 Components (specification table, component detail, quality-driver interactions, external integrations), 5 Models, 6 Refinements, Appendix A-E (public/sdd-architecture.js:419-1715). Parts kept per audience: all = introduction, drivers, decisions, components, models, refinements, A-E; delivery adds obligations; review adds summaries and assurance (public/sdd-architecture.js:1762-1766). Seen at runtime in sdd_architecture_{all,delivery,review}_nofig.md.
- Executive edition — PRODUCES a 2-page reading: what it is, requirements, drivers, decisions, shape, runtime, protection, and risks and open items; the delivery audience swaps drivers, decisions and protection for interfaces and obligations; the review audience drops shape and runtime (public/sdd-document.js:327, :376-397). Runtime: the executive edition lists THR-001 to THR-004, flags THR-002 and THR-003 as having no control linked, and counts 213 findings to fix and 350 items to confirm (sdd_executive_all_nofig.md).
- Traceability of the SDD to records — PRODUCES, mostly. Each section prints the IDs it rests on (drivers print 'Requirements it answers'; decisions print 'Answers: REQ…, QD…'; components print their responsibilities, requirements, controls, technologies and placement). There is a content-standard banner per section (public/sdd-architecture.js:1789-1798), open items listed by record in Appendix B, and provenance by chapter (Sol, imported, rules, person, AIW reference, confirmed) in Appendix E (public/sdd-document.js:5-6). The figure manifest carries a SHA-256 per figure plus a document fingerprint (JSON key figures.manifest[].sha256 in sdd_architecture_all.json). The SDD JSON carries structure, figures, openItems, content and provenance, but no passage-to-record map, and the HTML links only to headings, never to records (checked at runtime).
- Annexes — PRODUCES: the requirement register (md/csv/json/html, paged), the traceability matrix (csv/json, with separate 'named' and 'through the design' columns per layer), component specifications (md/html), the review registers (md/html), and the figures annex (md/html/zip with manifest/pdf) (worker.js:366, :373-389; public/sdd-annexes.js:1-17). The review registers hold: the framing including the Architecture approach, a requirement traceability register per requirement, architect review, the ARS, performance specification, component specs, delivery obligations, source findings and treatments, the connected model register, and every chapter register (public/review-domain.js:564). Runtime: annex_*.{md,csv,json,html,zip,pdf} all HTTP 200.
- ADRs — PRODUCES, partly. The Chapter 3 register, the SDD §3 decision log, per-decision entries and a trade-off analysis with arithmetic, sensitivity and dominance, labelled 'not a choice' (public/sdd-architecture.js:673-800), plus a MADR zip built in the browser, one derived file per decision (public/madr.js:178-196; public/output-documents.js:57). Runtime: the ADR-001 MADR, generated through madrText, carries context, decision drivers, options with good/bad points, consequences (from risks), confirmation (from assumptions), the linked requirements, and 'Derived from AIW record ADR-001 (revision 1…); the saved record is the authority'.
- Quality/NFR register — PRODUCES the Architecture Requirements Specification (ARS) in md/html/json (worker.js:541; public/ars-document.js:1-18). Per driver it gives code, category, stimulus, conditions, response, target, a 'Measurable: yes/no' verdict, verification, the reason for its priority, a candidate tactic, the trace to REQ/LR/APP/ADR/SEC and the runtime plans reached. It also lists conflicts, constraints (CON-001), assumptions (ASM-001), data invariants and the batch chain (seen in /home/user/hb-review/scratch/outputs-views/out/ars.md). Measurability is a text test (public/quality-domain.js:111-120).

## worked-example

**Question.** Can an architect carry a handbook worked example through AIW end to end? (Worked case: Applied_Solution_Architecture_Volume_10_Handbook › Complete reference architecture dossiers › Recoverable payment reference architecture (ASA-BP-PAY), plus Chapter 6 Recoverable payment architecture and operating blueprint (ASA-TX-006). It was recorded through the AIW API on a fresh blank project, project-df56d38a-099b-4c08-b279-d4510974b842, scratch DB /home/user/hb-review/scratch/worked-example/project.sqlite, command log /home/user/hb-review/scratch/worked-example/commands.jsonl, SDD /home/user/hb-review/scratch/worked-example/sdd.md.)

**Answer.** Mostly yes, but AIW cannot hold the case faithfully. 300 API commands created 186 records across all eleven chapters, and AIW refused 7 of them. Chapter 1 ended with no findings. The structure carried over: requirements with Given/When/Then acceptance, measurable quality scenarios, four decisions with their alternatives and rejection reasons, six components, contracts with idempotency keys, invariants and a lifecycle, threats and controls, two environments, plateaus and a migration record. Where the handbook goes beyond AIW's fields, AIW either forced free text, raised checks that were wrong for this design, or left facts out of the SDD:
- **Hard requirements.** AIW cannot mark a quality requirement as hard, so a hard failure is weighed instead of ruling the alternative out.
- **Separate reconciler.** The handbook's reconciler is flagged as an execution "conflict".
- **Open recovery targets.** RTO and RPO marked as open questions on a capability are silently dropped.
- **Local versus target.** Technology choices, capabilities and controls cannot be scoped to one environment, so the SDD shows the SQLite/Python teaching stack as the design's technology.
- **Facts missing from the SDD.** Decision review triggers and context, contract failure and retry terms, runbooks, the assumption register, threats on non-component targets and the first transition stage are not in the SDD body.
Final state: 16 source errors and 231 items to confirm, plus 5 Chapter 11 errors. About half of the source errors trace to AIW limits rather than to gaps in the handbook. Sol was not exercised (no provider configured); every record was entered by hand.

**Skeptic on the answer.** Four parts of the direct answer need correcting. (1) 'The handbook's reconciler is flagged as an execution conflict' is wrong. The conflict comes from linking IF-003 as IF-002's separate result contract. With discovery 'manual-reconciliation' ('Owned reconciliation against the authority', public/composition-manifest.js:65) and the provider as outcomeAuthorityId, I reproduced on doc_final.json that there are no blockers (architecture-applicability.js:52, :86-104). (2) 'Hard requirements… a hard failure is weighed' is only half right. AIW does rule out an alternative that breaks a hard constraint-kind driver (decision-reading.js:146-150, :178); it cannot make one measured quality driver hard. (3) 'AIW refused 7 commands' is accurate, but only 3 of the 7 reflect AIW limits: 'breaks' on a quality driver, the catalogue pattern id on a component, and the event-based review date. The other 4 were the analyst's input errors: a conflict naming drivers that do not exist, an invalid operating-model value, and two invalid result-contract enumerations (commands.jsonl). (4) 'About half of the source errors trace to AIW limits' is slightly high. The IF-002 conflict (point 1) and the 4 Ch7 implementation-obligation errors (TR-002–TR-005) were avoidable in AIW: the obligations could have been recorded as open, which has() accepts. That leaves about 7 of 16: the 4 unbound controls, the TC-001 RTO/RPO bug, QD-011, and ENV-002. The 'facts missing from the SDD' are missing only from the body; they appear in the SDD's Appendix D registers annex. The first transition stage was unprinted because no current-state plateau was recorded. The rest of the answer stands.

**What AIW does today.**

- Ch1 holds outcomes with measure/baseline/target, stakeholders and actors with personas, scope in/out, constraints, assumptions, journeys with journeyId and processKind, business rules (public/requirements-domain.js:18-29, :172-178). All 8 ASA-REQ-PAY requirements passed the acceptance and ambiguity checks, and Chapter 1 ended at 0 findings (requirements-domain.js:98-102).
- Ch2 quality drivers hold a three-part scenario (stimulus, conditions, response) and a measurable target: metric, operator, value, unit, window, verification (public/record-fields.js:126; public/quality-domain.js:120). A driver conflict with an owner and a resolution was recorded (quality-domain.js:278-280).
- Ch3 decisions hold at least 2 alternatives, per-driver effects with reason and evidence, rationale, rejection reasons and consequences (public/decisions-domain.js:166-196). The deterministic reading ruled out the two ADR-PAY-02 alternatives that break the hard constraint driver C1, printed dominance and sensitivity, and never chose (public/decision-reading.js:146-150, :178, :217).
- Ch4 responsibilities hold purpose, boundary, owner, group and requirement links, and connect to journey steps by 'serves' (public/logical-domain.js:196-213). Chapter 4 reached 0 errors once each responsibility was re-saved.
- Ch5 components hold a kind, allocations with scope, quality-property rows, served drivers and interactions with a failure policy (public/realisation-domain.js:224-247). The handbook's six components were recorded with 0 errors.
- Ch6 holds needs, capabilities (trust boundary, failure domain, continuity, recovery plan and owner), trust boundaries with a policy, and dependencies (public/technology-domain.js:289-340).
- Ch7 holds realizations with options, six obligation plans, capacity with its basis (Little's-law sizing recorded), driver-tied targets, and a recorded selection kept separate from external approval (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:96-111, :234-244).
- Ch8 holds contracts with correlation and idempotency keys, duplicate/failure/timeout/retry policies, authorisation and transport, and an execution agreement assessed against the governed composition method (public/architecture-applicability.js:70-110). It also holds money fields with a currency, a contract-driven status lifecycle and balance/uniqueness invariants (public/interfaces-domain.js:24-33, :146-158).
- Ch9 holds threats (category, priority, targets) and controls (enforcement, mechanism, failure mode, telemetry, verification plan, parameters), all with 0 errors (public/security-domain.js:79-99). An architect's accept is kept apart from governance approval (security-domain.js:127).
- Ch10 holds environments with a stage, zones with failure domain and boundaries, and plans with state mode, recovery strategy, fencing, backup, monitoring, runbook, rollout and rollback. It binds Ch9 controls to plans and flags unbound ones (public/runtime-domain.js:222, :304-337).
- Ch11 holds the narrative, plateaus, a migration record (from/to/scope/reconciliation/rollback/rehearsal) and delivery actions with typed verification (public/roadmap-domain.js:84-160; public/review-domain.js:186). Sol and agent accounts cannot send review commands (authorship.js:37-60).
- SDD §2 prints each driver's scenario and target; §3 prints the decision log, per-driver effects, alternatives with why-not and the trade-off analysis; §4 prints quality properties, invariants, lifecycles and external integrations. Decisions print as 'Architect accepted · No current approval', which respects the doctrine. The ARS carries the driver conflict and the constraints (public/sdd-architecture.js:535-590, :673-800, :821-1060).

**Gaps.**

- **[high | 2 Drivers / 3 Decisions | effort M] Hard quality requirements cannot rule out an alternative; a hard failure is weighed and compensated**
  - AIW: Sending 'violates' for ADR-002's 'Direct synchronous dispatch' against QD-004 was refused: '“Breaks it” is for a constraint… record as tension' (public/decisions-domain.js:195). Only constraint-kind drivers get hard force (public/decision-reading.js:168-169, :146-150). A quality driver has only High/Medium/Low (record-fields.js:126). Result: ALT-004 scored −7 and was ranked '3 of 3', not ruled out, in SDD §3. ADR-003's alternatives were ruled out only because a separate constraint driver C1 was added.
  - Handbook: Recoverable payment reference architecture › Requirements and measurement: ASA-REQ-PAY-01..06 and -08 'Constraint and owner. Hard', and -07 'Preference after correctness'. Reusable design templates › Requirement and measurement contract: 'Constraint status: Hard, preference or assumption; never compensate a hard failure with a preference score.' Decision records › Accept durably before dispatch lists the direct-dispatch alternative.
  - Recommendation: Add a person-set constraint status (hard / preference / assumption) to quality drivers. Let 'breaks' apply to hard quality drivers, and have the deterministic reading rule out such alternatives. Keep knowledge and Sol out of setting the status: a person states it and the rules read it.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Partly true, and less severe than stated (medium, not high). AIW can rule out an alternative that breaks a hard requirement. A constraint-kind driver is hard unless every Ch1 constraint it links is Should or Could (public/decision-reading.js:146-150). Such an alternative goes into `breaks` and is excluded from the eligible set (decision-reading.js:168, :178-179). The analyst used this route for ADR-003 through C1 and could have done the same for ADR-002/QD-004. What AIW cannot do is make one measured quality driver hard: 'breaks' is refused on non-constraint drivers (decisions-domain.js:195), and drivers of other kinds are 'described and linked, not measured' (quality-domain.js:161). Holding a hard requirement with a target therefore takes two drivers. The handbook citations hold (Recoverable payment reference architecture › Requirements and measurement: 'Constraint and owner. Hard'; Reusable design templates › Architecture decision record: 'Feasibility gates — Pass, fail or unknown for each mandatory condition').
- **[high | 8 Interfaces & data | effort M] Execution check reports the handbook's separate reconciler as a conflict**
  - AIW: Linking IF-002 'Dispatch an external effect' (caller: Dispatch worker) to its outcome contract IF-003 'Reconcile outcome evidence' (caller: Reconciler) raises the error 'Bind result discovery to the original caller; an intermediary needs its own supported boundary' (public/architecture-applicability.js:110). SDD §4 'Execution agreements' prints 'conflict — declared mechanisms do not compose'.
  - Handbook: Recoverable payment reference architecture (intro): 'Worker finalisation requires the current generation; authoritative reconciliation can close the obligation without owning that worker lease.' Interface contracts: ASA-IF-PAY-03 Producer is Dispatch worker; ASA-IF-PAY-04 Producer is Reconciler.
  - Recommendation: Add a reviewed declaration of a recovery participant inside the same owning boundary (for example an outcome-discoverer id with a justification), or add a reconciler recipe to the governed composition method through its own review. Do not loosen the rule for undeclared intermediaries.
  - Skeptic: DOES NOT HOLD — Refuted. The conflict comes from the analyst's modelling choice, not from an AIW limit. AIW has a declared mechanism for exactly this case: discovery 'manual-reconciliation' = 'Owned reconciliation against the authority' (public/composition-manifest.js:65). With outcomeAuthorityId set to the provider (architecture-applicability.js:86-89), the outcome-caller rule never runs. That rule fires only when a separate result contract is linked, which is the analyst's choice of outcomeContractId 'if-003' (architecture-applicability.js:52, :90-104). I verified this by running assessContractExecution on IF-002 from doc_final.json. As recorded: state 'excluded', blocker 'outcome-caller' (value obj-004, expected obj-002). With discovery='manual-reconciliation', outcomeAuthorityId=obj-003 and the outcome-group fields removed: no blockers. State is then 'needs-context' only because execution.environmentId was never set. What remains is narrow and low severity: AIW cannot verify IF-003 as IF-002's result contract when the callers differ. Recommend documenting the manual-reconciliation route; no new declaration is needed.
- **[high | 6 Logical technology | effort S] RTO/RPO recorded as open on a capability are silently dropped; per-data-class RPO cannot be held**
  - AIW: saveCapability passes CAPABILITY_FIELDS to patchOpen (public/technology-domain.js:327). That list omits recoveryMinutes and lossMinutes (:316), although CAPABILITY_OPEN (:276) and has() (:78) accept them. Sending open {recoveryMinutes, lossMinutes} (both 'open-question' and 'not-stated') saved nothing, while alternateDomain's open state was kept. The 'recovery-plan' error stays on TC-001 (:244). There is one lossMinutes per capability.
  - Handbook: Chapter 6 Recoverable payment architecture and operating blueprint › Implementation and parameters: 'Recovery point objective — Define separately for accepted intents and audit evidence'. Deployment and recovery › Core state (ASA-DEP-PAY-02).
  - Recommendation: Bug fix: pass CAPABILITY_OPEN to patchOpen. Then allow recovery targets as rows per data class, like the Ch7/Ch10 target rows, so accepted intents and audit evidence can carry separate RPOs.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The bug is confirmed. saveCapability calls patchOpen with CAPABILITY_FIELDS (public/technology-domain.js:327). That list omits recoveryMinutes and lossMinutes (:316), while CAPABILITY_OPEN (:276) and has() (:78) accept them, and cleanOpen silently filters to the listed fields (technology-domain.js:55). The 'recovery-plan' check then fails (:244). One overstatement: per-data-class RPO can be held. Ch7 realization and Ch10 plan target rows accept up to 24 rows of kind 'data-loss', each with value, unit, window and basis text (public/driver-links.js:22, :41-50), so separate RPOs for accepted intents and audit evidence fit there. Only the Ch6 capability is single-valued. The handbook citation holds (Chapter 6 › Implementation and parameters: 'Recovery point objective — Define separately for accepted intents and audit evidence').
- **[high | 7 Technology realization / 6 / 10 | effort L] Local realisation and target candidate cannot coexist; SDD presents the teaching stack as the design**
  - AIW: Each realization keeps one preferred/recorded option (public/technology-realisation-domain.js:96-111). A target-candidate option with no product named raises a 'product' error. SDD §4 Technologies lines print 'SQLite 3 (Current architect selection)' and 'Python standard library http.server'. 'reviewed relational database service' and 'Target candidate' appear 0 times in sdd.md. Capabilities and controls have no environment scope: the local-only simulator capability gives 'missing-dependency' in the target environment (runtime-domain.js:224), and target-only controls give four 'unbound-SEC' errors in the local environment (runtime-domain.js:222). The SDD deployment figure falls back to the local environment because the target has no placements (public/sdd-c4.js:382-387).
  - Handbook: Recoverable payment reference architecture › Deployment and recovery (ASA-DEP-PAY-01..04): each unit has a 'Local realisation' (for example 'SQLite core.sqlite3') and a 'Target candidate' (for example 'Reviewed relational database service'), plus 'Validation required'. Evidence boundary paragraph.
  - Recommendation: Make the technology selection per environment (or let a Ch10 plan name the Ch7 option it runs). Add environment applicability to capabilities and controls. Print the technology per environment in the SDD, and let a person choose which environment the deployment figure shows.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The core claim holds and is stronger than reported. Even the obvious workaround fails: separate local and target realizations, each in its own environment. The capability-to-realization mapping has no environment scope. dependenciesOf adds a dependency to every realization of the capability, and one with no plan in that environment becomes a null target (public/runtime-model.js:44). That raises 'missing-dependency' in each environment (runtime-domain.js:224). Two details are wrong. (1) The 'product' error on a target option with no product is avoidable: the product can be marked open (option patchOpen over OPTION_FIELDS, technology-realisation-domain.js:195), and has() accepts the open state (:207). The analyst left product '' with no open state. (2) The deployment-figure fallback is not an AIW limit. deploymentChoice picks a production-stage environment that has placements (public/sdd-c4.js:384-386), and ENV-002 had none recorded. Options not selected appear only in the registers annex ('reviewed relational database' appears 9 times in registers.md and 0 in sdd.md).
- **[high | 3 Decisions / 11 SDD | effort S] No review-trigger or scoped-status field on decisions; context, assumptions and triggers are missing from the SDD**
