Add fields for approver authority (role and delegated limit), compensating controls and what they do not address, and expiry. Keep 'uncovered' open, as 'accepted, awaiting independent approval', until an independent approval exists, and raise an error when an accepted Critical/High risk passes its expiry. ## enterprise-alignment

**Question.** Does AIW connect a solution architecture to enterprise and business architecture as the Enterprise Architecture and Business Architecture handbooks prescribe? Specifically: capabilities and capability maps, value streams, processes, organisation and stakeholders, objectives and KPIs, principles, enterprise standards and reference architectures, technology radar and approved products, architecture governance (boards, design authority, waivers, compliance assessment), portfolio and application inventory, and reuse of enterprise building blocks. Can a design be checked for conformance to enterprise principles and standards, and is a waiver recorded with a person's authority?

**Answer.** Only partly. The connection AIW does well is a governed organisation library: entries of kind standard, pattern, reference-architecture, technology and so on go through review by a second person, a signed release and a reviewed adoption into each project. Projects can also declare dependencies on shared enterprise services and get told when those dependencies drift. But a design cannot be checked for conformance to enterprise standards, principles or reference architectures. Adopted standard and reference-architecture entries cannot be applied to any record: workspace.guidance refuses them, as the live run showed. No chapter check, finding or SDD section reads them; they reach only Sol's advisory packets, through word-overlap retrieval. Business architecture has no first-class records: no capability map, value stream, organisation unit or decision-rights record, KPI measurement contract or application inventory. The nearest things are requirement 'capability theme' groups, Chapter 1 outcomes with free-text measures and persona-style stakeholders. On import, an ArchiMate business Capability becomes a Chapter 6 technology capability and a Principle becomes a Chapter 1 constraint. There is no waiver record. The nearest is a Chapter 11 'Accepted design limitation' disposition, which keeps the signed-in actor, a typed reviewer name and a reference. It does not record the waived rule, scope, authority mandate, compensating controls or expiry, and with four eyes off by default the design's own author can record it (shown live). Independent approval is enforced only on baseline governance evidence.

**Skeptic on the answer.** Five statements need correcting. (1) 'A design cannot be checked for conformance to enterprise standards, principles or reference architectures' is too strong. AIW does it partly, through a person. A standard or principle transcribed as a Chapter 1 constraint (Must makes it hard), with a Chapter 2 constraint driver, makes every Chapter 3 alternative and Chapter 7 option record supports, tension or breaks with a reason and evidence. A hard break is an error that blocks acceptance (decisions-domain.js:18-20, :127-130; technology-realisation-domain.js:209, :237; decision-reading.js:146-151). I verified this in memory with AIW's own functions (/home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-ea-ba/inmem.mjs). What is missing is the link from the adopted signed entry to these records, drift reopening when the entry changes, and reference-architecture tailoring. (2) 'There is no waiver record; the nearest is Accepted design limitation' misses Chapter 9 risk acceptance. It records the 'accept' treatment, an owner, the residual risk and a next-review date. An overdue date raises 'risk-due'; an acceptance without governance raises 'risk-approval'. Governance records approved, rejected or deferred (security-domain.js:126-130, :177-178; verified in /home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-ea-ba/inmem-sec.mjs). Its reviewer is typed, with no signed-in independence. Also, a transcribed-standard 'breaks' or 'hard-constraint' finding does exist and reaches Chapter 11 for treatment (review-domain.js:107, :415), so a waiver need not attach to an unrelated finding. (3) 'Adopted standard and reference-architecture entries cannot be applied to any record' is slightly overstated. Any entry kind can carry a shared operational identity (organization-service.js:74). Records can then declare a scoped dependency on it, with drift warnings (enterprise-references.js:22-35). This is a dependency link, not conformance. (4) 'Business architecture has no first-class records' misses the governed 'Enterprise capability' shared identity (enterprise-references.js:5), which records in Chapters 1 and 4-10 can reference. It also misses the project-local Chapter 8 parties with 'legacy system' kind and retiring status, which feed the Chapter 11 migration finding (interfaces-domain.js:40; roadmap-domain.js:261-273). (5) Adopted entries reach more than Sol's packets: they are also shown in the knowledge graph (knowledge-graph.js:58-62) and the Chapter 3 guidance list (workbench-ui.js:103). Both are display only. The rest of the answer is accurate. The organisation library's second-person review, signed release and adoption are confirmed. The ArchiMate Capability-to-Chapter 6 misfiling is confirmed (model-interpreter.js:438; journey-drafter.js:677-683). Four eyes is off by default (changes-domain.js:211-213). Independent approval is enforced among design approvals only for baseline governance (review-domain.js:266-274, :427). All cited handbook headings and quotations were found in Enterprise_Architecture_Handbook.md and Business_Architecture_Handbook.md. The scratch server was started on port 4637 with a scratch database and stopped by PID.

**What AIW does today.**

- Paths are relative to /home/user/AIW-Brain/AIW-V5-Local-Source. Live checks used the scratch scripts /home/user/hb-review/scratch/ea-ba-gov/probe.mjs and /home/user/hb-review/scratch/ea-ba-gov/archimate-probe.mjs, run against a server on a scratch database that has since been stopped by PID.
- Organisation workspaces have the roles administrator, editor, reviewer and reader. Library kinds include standard, pattern, reference-architecture, technology, control, runbook, the record templates and component (organization-service.js:17, :61-65; organization-ui.js:13).
- A library entry must keep the exact source location, passage and revision, plus conditions, limitations, lifecycle and an optional ISO review-due date. Its history is retained (organization-service.js:70-75).
- Review by a second person: the author or last editor is refused with 403, and approval needs conditions and limitations (organization-service.js:79-80). Live run: self-review returned 403 'A different authenticated reviewer must assess this entry.' Agent accounts cannot review or release (worker.js:435).
- Releases are signed with HMAC and contain only independently reviewed entries. An entry overdue for review blocks the release (organization-service.js:20-23, :85-87).
- Adoption into a project ('install') copies entries into the project catalogue as approved, with their source evidence (organization-service.js:89-97). Eligibility is re-checked on every load: membership, signature, local source drift, withdrawal and overdue review (organization-service.js:132-147). Live run: four entries were adopted with 'Verified signed release'.
- Shared enterprise dependencies cover four asset types: Shared service, Shared API, Data platform and Enterprise capability (enterprise-references.js:5). A consumer record declares scope, contract, expected version and criticality. The preview refuses unapproved, retired, changed or withdrawn contracts (enterprise-references.js:22-35). The server records actor, reason and history (organization-service.js:46-59). Live run: a dependency of APP-001 on the notification API was saved, read as 'Current declaration', with actor 'architect'.
- Dependency currency is re-derived on each read: consumer changed, release changed, contract changed, withdrawn or retired. A stale dependency raises the warning 'Review changed shared-service dependency' (enterprise-references.js:10-21; guidance-record.js:19).
- Cross-project impact of a shared service is listed only for projects the reader owns or can access (organization-service.js:37-44).
- Shared dependencies appear in SDD section 4 under 'Shared enterprise dependencies' (sdd-architecture.js:886-888) and as shared-service nodes with issues in the model (architecture-model.js:50, :220-226).
- The enterprise-references test suite exercises review, signed release, compare-and-swap adoption, reopening on consumer, source and contract drift, and withdrawal (enterprise-references-validate.mjs:33-101).
- Adopted technology, control and runbook entries can be adapted, through a reviewed preview, into a Chapter 7 option, a Chapter 9 control or a Chapter 10 runbook. Requirement, scenario, decision and document templates draft records (workbench-domain.js:62-75).
- Reuse of enterprise building blocks: component library entries in project or organisation releases are proposed through the reviewed realisation.candidate path. Library content 'enters no score, no hard constraint and no conformance reading' (component-library.js:1-11; realisation-domain.js:334-339).
- Adopted organisation guidance, including standards and reference architectures, reaches Sol's packet only by word-overlap retrieval, top four entries, with the posture 'Applicability and project adoption require architect judgement' (organization-knowledge.js:2-4; intelligence-context.js:198).
- Chapter 1 business context: the outcome type has free-text problem, measure, baseline, target and by-when fields. Stakeholders and actors have a category (staff role, partner, customer, external organisation, regulator) and six persona fields. Business rules carry authority, instrument and section, and link to Chapter 8 data and Chapter 9 controls (requirements-domain.js:18-29, :172-178).
- Requirement groups can be of kind functional area, capability theme, module, sub-module, domain or user group (requirement-groups.js:27-29).
- Governance evidence records: decision.governance holds status, reviewer or review body, and evidence (record-fields.js:132). techrealisation.approval holds reviewer, authority, evidence and confirmation of an existing external approval (record-fields.js:161; technology-realisation-domain.js:244). review.governance per baseline has the outcomes Approved, Approved with conditions, Rejected and Withdrawn (review-domain.js:74), and requires a signed-in approver who is not the review author; agents never count (review-domain.js:266-274).
- Chapter 11 treatments of findings: Fix before review, Carry as action, Accepted design limitation, Not applicable, and Source silent (review-domain.js:72). A disposition stores at, actor, typed reviewer, rationale and reference (review-domain.js:415), and can treat a whole cause (review-domain.js:350-363).
- Sol drafts and agent accounts cannot send review, disposition, governance or knowledge-release commands, and cannot claim a review (authorship.js:37-62).
- The domain lens (core banking) is a deterministic concern checklist written in code. It is an item to confirm and 'never knowledge' (domain-lens.js:1-12; lenses/core-banking.js header).
- ArchiMate import maps Capability and BusinessFunction to 'capability', ValueStream to 'process', Principle to 'constraint' and Goal to 'outcome' (model-interpreter.js:434-438; confirmed live). The drafter turns a 'capability' element into a Chapter 6 technology capability (journey-drafter.js:506-507, :677-683).

**Gaps.**

- **[critical | Cross-cutting (Chapter 3 decisions, Chapters 5-10 records, Chapter 11 Validate and SDD) | effort L] A design cannot be checked for conformance to adopted enterprise standards or reference architectures**
  - AIW: AIW CANNOT do this; it happens ONLY THROUGH SOL, as advice. standard, pattern and reference-architecture entries can be adopted (organization-service.js:17) and are listed in Chapter 3's 'Reusable chapter guidance', but the only action offered is Review / revise (workbench-ui.js:103). workspace.guidance refuses them: 'Use this entry through its chapter template or architectural reasoning workflow' (workbench-domain.js:74); the live run got HTTP 400 for both a standard and a reference architecture. None of decisions-domain.js, technology-domain.js, technology-realisation-domain.js, security-domain.js, runtime-domain.js or interfaces-domain.js reads organisation entries (0 references). Live run: 616 inherited and 11 final findings, none naming the adopted STD-SEC-01 or RA-02. 'Conformance' elsewhere means delivery observations (model-conformance.js:4-8) or OpenAPI and JSON Schema checks (standards-domain.js:22). Standards reach only Sol packets, by word-overlap retrieval (organization-knowledge.js:4; intelligence-context.js:198). There is no tailoring or applicability record for a reference architecture.
  - Handbook: Enterprise Architecture Handbook › Approval Theatre and Standards without Enforcement: trace each rule through authority, applicability, implementation evidence and response; a 'decision-to-evidence index' and 'conformance sampling'. Enterprise Architecture Handbook › Conditional Reference Architectures: 'Bind each mandatory condition to an evidence requirement. A tailoring record states which profile applies and why; an unmet condition is a gap or disqualifier'. Enterprise Architecture Handbook › Decision rights exceptions and architecture assurance: match assurance to the claim.
  - Recommendation: Add a person-authored conformance assertion in Chapter 11, with a Chapter 3 view. For each adopted entry that is released, eligible and was verified by a second person, a person records 'applies', or 'not applicable' with a reason. For each mandatory condition, the person names the design records that satisfy it and the evidence. Deterministic findings are raised when: an applicable entry has no assertion; an asserted record's fingerprint changes; or the release, or the entry's eligibility, changes. These findings are treated in Chapter 11 and printed in a new SDD 'Enterprise conformance' section. Sol may propose candidate mappings only as proposals marked sol-draft; a person confirms them. Only released, four-eyes knowledge feeds the check, never candidate or discovery knowledge. The organisation-entry authority text then changes from 'no conformance reading' to 'conformance as asserted by a person'.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Holds for adopted library entries, but 'AIW CANNOT do this; ONLY THROUGH SOL' is wrong. AIW does it PARTLY, through a person, by a path the analyst missed. A person can transcribe a standard as a Chapter 1 constraint; Must makes it hard (decision-reading.js:146-151). It is linked through a Chapter 2 constraint driver (quality-domain.js:67, :167). Every Chapter 3 alternative that names the driver then records supports, tension or breaks, with a reason and evidence (decisions-domain.js:18-20, :195). Breaking a hard constraint is an error that blocks acceptance and approval (decisions-domain.js:127-130). The same holds for Chapter 7 options: ASSESSMENT_EFFECTS at technology-realisation-domain.js:48 and :237, and the 'hard-constraint' error at :209. I verified this in memory with AIW's own applyCanonical and decisionFindings on a bank-payment project (CON-001 Must, then QD-007 constraint, then ADR-001 'violates'): it gave 'breaks', error, 'The working choice breaks a hard constraint' (/home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-ea-ba/inmem.mjs). Business rules also carry authority, instrument and section, and link to the Chapter 9 control that enforces them (requirements-domain.js:177-178). Released governed claims with a typed architectureUnit are matched to Chapter 7 options deterministically, not by Sol (architecture-knowledge.js:20-35; product-choice.js:85). They are shown, never counted. The real gaps are these. The transcribed constraint has no link to the signed organisation entry, so a new release or withdrawal of the entry reopens nothing. There is no tailoring record for a reference architecture. The recommendation should extend the existing path from Chapter 1 constraint through Chapter 2 driver to assessment, citing the adopted entry and reopening on release drift, rather than add a parallel 'conformance assertion' authority path. AGENTS.md says 'do not patch old and new authority paths side by side'.
- **[critical | Chapter 11 Review & realize | effort M] No waiver or dispensation record with waived rule, scope, approving authority, compensating controls and expiry**
  - AIW: AIW only PARTLY does this. The nearest record is the 'Accepted design limitation' disposition (review-domain.js:72). It stores only treatment, rationale, typed reviewer, reference, at and actor (review-domain.js:415; record-fields.js:209). It has no waived standard, scope, interval, revocation, compensating control or remediation owner, and no expiry; the map notes 'Accepted limitations have no expiry' (part-3 X2). Four eyes is off by default (changes-domain.js:211-213). Live run: RF-001 'Accepted design limitation' was recorded by actor 'architect', the project author, with the typed reviewer 'Chief Architect (typed name)'. It had to be attached to an unrelated finding (C1:acceptance:REQ-001), because no non-conformance finding exists to waive. Independent approval is enforced only for baseline governance (review-domain.js:266-274).
  - Handbook: Enterprise Architecture Handbook › Decision rights exceptions and architecture assurance: 'An exception should name the waived condition, covered scope, effective interval, approving authority, compensating controls, owner, expiry and exit plan'. Enterprise Architecture Handbook › Time Bounded Exceptions and Compensating Controls: half-open interval, revocation, exposure ceiling; exception authority, control owner, remediation owner and obligation owner; expiry stops new use but keeps obligations. Enterprise Architecture Handbook › Time bounded exception with retained accountability: 'No silent renewal'. Business Architecture Handbook › Evidence bearing Exceptions and Waivers.
  - Recommendation: Add an exception-grant record in Chapter 11. It must link to a conformance finding from the first gap, and to the standard's entry id and release. Fields: waived condition; covered records; start date (inclusive) and end date (exclusive); revocation; exposure limit; compensating controls as links to Chapter 9 controls; a remediation action as a link to a review.action; approving authority name and mandate reference. The signed-in approver must differ from the design author whatever the fourEyes setting, as review.governance already requires. Agents are refused. A deterministic expiry check reopens the finding. Renewal is a new grant that keeps the old one. Accepted design limitation stays for non-standard findings.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The core claim holds: no record has a waived condition, interval, authority mandate or compensating-control fields, and four eyes is off by default (changes-domain.js:211-213; review-domain.js:337). Two statements are wrong. (1) 'The nearest is Accepted design limitation' misses Chapter 9 risk acceptance. security.risk with treatment 'accept' requires an owner, the residual risk and an ISO next-review date (security-domain.js:177). An overdue date raises 'risk-due'. An acceptance without approved governance raises 'risk-approval' (security-domain.js:126-130). security.governance records approved, rejected or deferred, and a rejection raises an error (security-domain.js:128, :178). Verified in memory: RR-001 'accept' with nextReview 2026-01-31 gave 'risk-approval' and 'risk-due' warnings (/home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-ea-ba/inmem-sec.mjs). Its reviewer is typed only; it needs no signed-in independent approver and covers threats, not enterprise standards. (2) 'No non-conformance finding exists to waive' is false once the standard is transcribed as a hard constraint. The 'breaks' error (decisions-domain.js:130) and the 'hard-constraint' error (technology-realisation-domain.js:209) are inherited into Chapter 11 (review-domain.js:107, :110), where review.disposition can treat them (review-domain.js:415). The recommendation should build on the security.risk and security.governance pattern and target these existing findings.

The only capability construct is a requirement group of kind 'Capability theme' (requirement-groups.js:27-29) and an imported 'capability theme' attribute. ## operability-and-delivery

**Question.** Does AIW carry the design through to operability and delivery as the handbooks prescribe? This covers SLOs and error budgets, observability, runbooks and incident response, capacity and performance, CI/CD and release strategies, IaC and configuration management, environment strategy and parity, test strategy, operational acceptance and handover, the support model, and the handoff from architecture to engineering (design detail, module structure, coding standards, ADRs).

**Answer.** Only partly. AIW carries the design as far as an operating sketch written by the architect. Chapter 10 requires text on every runtime plan for monitoring, runbook, rollout, rollback, scaling, readiness, network policy, capacity basis and recovery (public/runtime-domain.js:239-244). It also checks failure domains, standby and state protection as structure, simulates the loss of a zone, and keeps design reviews apart from live exercises. Chapter 11 turns obligations into owned delivery actions with typed metric or assertion checks tied to an environment and a release. It also gates an implementation handoff behind a second person's approval and exports delivery, verification and operating-specification JSON.

Several areas are only partial:
- **Service objectives:** targets tied to drivers exist, but there is no denominator and no error budget.
- **Observability and runbooks:** required, but stored as free text.
- **Capacity:** the desk arithmetic is strong, but it has no rollout-overlap envelope.
- **Release strategy:** written as text, or as a Chapter 3 decision that is never linked to the runtime plan.
- **Testing:** evidence is recorded claim by claim, and there is no test strategy.

AIW cannot model:
- CI/CD pipelines, artifact identity and promotion;
- infrastructure as code and configuration management;
- feature flags and error budgets;
- typed signals, alerts and dashboards;
- incident roles and support tiers;
- environment contracts and parity matrices;
- an operational acceptance or go-live decision;
- code module structure and coding standards.

A live clinic-appointments export confirmed two more problems. The SDD's architecture edition contains none of the operating text, and the drafted journey creates one "production design" environment per component, which raises Chapter 10 errors by construction. Sol only drafts prose into these fields. Evidence, acceptance and handoff stay with a person, which matches the doctrine and the handbooks' rule that a design or a local test is never production acceptance.

**Skeptic on the answer.** The direct answer is broadly right but overstates some absences and misattributes one point. (1) The claim that a release strategy decided in Chapter 3 is 'never linked to the runtime plan' is too strong. A plan reaches its asset's decisions, which the Chapter 10 register prints per plan (public/runtime-domain.js:343). The Chapter 2 tactic reading also matches Blue-Green, Canary, Rollback, CI/CD and IaC playbook cues in the plan's rollout, rollback, runbook and monitoring text (public/design-reasoning.js:176-192, :236-243). It is a text reading, not a typed link. (2) Under 'cannot model … feature flags and error budgets', the answer should add that the released catalogue holds PAT-FEATURE-FLAG, PAT-ERROR-BUDGET, PAT-SLO-BASED-OPERATIONS, PAT-INFRASTRUCTURE-AS-CODE, PAT-GITOPS and the canary, blue-green, rolling and shadow deployment patterns (public/brain-catalogue.js:20, :42, :83-85, :109, :208, :218-220, :253). The Chapter 3 alternative picker offers all of them (public/record-fields.js:131, :431-434). AIW cannot model them, but it can record them as decisions. (3) The answer omits AIW's read-only delivery connectors. GitHub Actions workflow runs arrive as test-result records carrying the head SHA as release; runtime-json, Jira and Azure work items are also read (connection-adapters.js:4, :36-39). (4) 'The SDD's architecture edition contains none of the operating text' is true of the body. However, the SDD registers annex (assembleSDD, public/review-domain.js:564, served at annex=registers, worker.js:385) carries the full Chapter 10 register, including monitoring, runbook, rollout and rollback. (5) One-environment-per-component is the journey-draft path (public/journey-drafter.js:433-450 with public/guided-creation.js:75), confirmed independently on clinic-appointments. The grouped architecture drafter already creates one environment per stage for all components (public/architecture-drafter.js:984-995). (6) 'Sol only drafts prose into these fields' is imprecise. The Chapter 10 journey draft is deterministic AIW drafting (provenance mode 'deterministic'), not Sol; Sol's language-model path into these fields is the field spark. (7) Gap 14 is refuted. Reusable runbooks and standards already have a four-eyes, signed organisation-release path (organization-service.js:17, :79-97; public/guidance-record.js:18). The handbook citation for 'Map responsibilities into modules' is SAD 05, not SAD 06. All other handbook headings I checked exist under the cited chapters. My scratch scripts are in /home/user/hb-review/scratch/skeptic-ops/.

**What AIW does today.**

- Chapter 10 runtime plan fields: owner ('Service / on-call owner'), releaseReference, min/max replicas, capacityBasis, scalingPolicy, readiness, networkPolicy, stateMode, recoveryStrategy, recoveryPlan, fencing, backupPlan, recovery and data-loss minutes, monitoring, runbook, rollout, rollback, securityEnforcement, controlIds, target rows and batch windows — public/record-fields.js:192
- The operability check raises an error when monitoring, runbook, rollout or rollback is missing: 'Make RUN-nnn operable' — public/runtime-domain.js:241. Scaling, readiness and network-policy checks are at :239. Capacity basis is a warning ('Replica counts are not proof of throughput or failover capacity') at :240. Recovery and state protection are at :242-243. Standby in an independent failure domain is at :246.
- Environment stages are fixed to Production, Pre-production, Recovery and Development design — public/runtime-domain.js:21. The stage is only checked for validity and printed (:310, :343); no finding reads it.
- Chapter 10 milestones keep the design review separate from live exercises ('Design review remains separate from live exercises'). Every environment needs current zone and failure-domain walkthroughs — public/runtime-domain.js:277-285. The runtime-validate.mjs:115 check asserts 'A simulation is not external exercise evidence'.
- A failed exercise reopens the handoff as an error. Open exercise evidence travels into the final review — runtime-validate.mjs:134-135
- Plan targets are typed as availability, recovery-time, data-loss, latency, throughput or capacity, each with operator, value, unit, window, basis and confirmation, and tied to a Chapter 2 driver — public/driver-links.js:22-49. A target that falls short of its driver is a 'target-driver' warning (public/runtime-domain.js:236).
- Chapter 2 quality drivers carry metric, operator, value, unit, window and measurement (verification) and a measurability test. Deployability and testability are among the concerns — public/quality-domain.js:22-23, :45, :111-120
- Anti-pattern ANTI-OBSERVABILITY-AS-AFTERTHOUGHT fires for a placed plan that carries a High driver and records no monitoring — public/design-spec.js:279-281. The Pattern DNA observability question is decided in Chapter 6 as a need — public/pattern-dna.js:165-177
- Chapter 8 contracts carry correlationKey, contractVersion, compatibility, timeout, retry and failure policies — public/record-fields.js:165
- Chapter 7 seeded observability options (OpenTelemetry with Prometheus and Grafana, or a managed APM) — public/technology-realisation-domain.js:125, :135
- The Chapter 10 workbench offers 'Capacity sensitivity', 'Compare environments', 'Rehearse a rollout' and 'Prepare operating specification' — public/workbench-ui.js:107. The functions are capacitySensitivity, compareEnvironments and rolloutWalkthrough ('Design rehearsal; no infrastructure is changed') — public/architecture-analysis.js:43-58
- The aiw.operating-spec.v1 export (plan, environment, placements, bound controls, unresolved Chapter 10 findings) requires a reviewed note. Its posture reads 'Engineering design input. Not an executable or approved infrastructure deployment.' — public/workbench-actions.js:36
- Reusable project-library runbooks: an approved entry with a source hash can be adapted into a plan's runbook through a reviewed change, and stale sources are rejected — public/workbench-domain.js:69-71, :183. Tested in development-validate.mjs:20
- Chapter 7 migration and exit procedure (trigger, sequence, data continuity, stop criteria, fallback, verification, decommission) — public/workbench-domain.js:78-81. Tested in development-validate.mjs:20
- The review desk's 'What it takes' turns a load objective into per-part replicas and connections using Little's law, with every assumption named — public/desk-capacity.js:1-10, :221. SCALE-NO-TARGET, SCALE-UNDER-PROVISIONED, SCALE-LATENCY-BUDGET and SCALE-UNCONFIRMED join the Chapter 10 checks — :375, :503-528

**Gaps.**

- **[high | 11 Review & realize (SDD) / 5 component specification | effort S] The SDD and the component specification omit the operating design**
  - AIW: The architecture edition prints only placement counts, start-up needs and the deployment diagram (public/sdd-architecture.js:1028-1030). No reference to monitoring, runbook, rollout or rollback exists in sdd-architecture.js, sdd-document.js or component-sheet.js. Their only display is the Model-tab dossier (public/model-dossier.js:100). A live clinic-appointments export (scratch/ops-delivery/sdd.md, 300 KB) had 0 matches for monitoring, runbook, rollout, rollback, alert or release. The component specification's 'Runtime (Chapter 10)' section gives only placements, state and recovery time (public/design-spec.js:100-106). The full Chapter 10 register is a separate annex export.
  - Handbook: Software_Application_Design_Handbook › SAD 29 › 'Include an operating design' (ownership, support paths, alerts, escalation, backup restoration, production acceptance, runbooks with stop conditions). SAD 29 › 'Create an implementation specification index' (a Deployment row: artifact, config, roles, budgets, drain). Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › TQ C10 › 'Produce a handover that another team can use'.

Build it deterministically from the Chapter 10 plans: targets and the drivers they serve, monitoring and alert ownership, runbook, rollout and rollback, recovery strategy and RTO/RPO assumptions, bound controls, and the state of design and exercise evidence. Mark open and placeholder values in Appendix B. The SDD stays a projection of the records and grants no approval.

I found no read of monitoring, runbook, rollout or rollback in sdd-architecture.js, sdd-document.js, component-sheet.js or component-spec-document.js. Three corrections. (1) The operating text does reach an SDD output. The registers annex (assembleSDD, public/review-domain.js:564, section 'Detailed chapter registers', which calls each CHAPTERS export) includes exportRuntimeMarkdown. That register prints Monitoring and alerts, Runbook, Rollout and Rollback for every plan (public/runtime-domain.js:343). It is served at /api/export?chapter=11&annex=registers (worker.js:385) and linked from Appendix D (public/sdd-architecture.js:1698-1705). So the gap is 'not in the body', not 'absent from the SDD'. (2) The component specification's Runtime section is public/component-spec-document.js:121, with tier() at :34. The cited public/design-spec.js:100-106 is the open-gaps list, not that section. (3) The component sheet also carries scalingPolicy, capacityBasis, standby, backup, recovery and loss (public/component-sheet.js:203-204), so it holds more than placements, state and recovery time.
- **[high | 10 Deployment / runtime | effort M] Environment strategy: stage is a label only, environment contracts and parity are missing, and the drafted journey creates one 'production design' environment per component**
  - AIW: There are four fixed stages (public/runtime-domain.js:21). No finding reads the stage (only :310 validation and :343 printing), so a Development environment's plans must meet production checks. The environment record holds only title, purpose, owner, basis, stage and assets (public/record-fields.js:189-204). compareEnvironments diffs 13 plan fields and states that it 'does not prove either environment is deployed or operationally equivalent' (public/architecture-analysis.js:43-47). The Chapter 10 journey draft makes '<component> production design' per component (public/journey-drafter.js:433-450). Live clinic run: ENV-001 to ENV-006 each scope one component, giving 6 missing-dependency errors and 6 'unscoped' warnings.
  - Handbook: DevOps_Master › Chapter 05 › 'An environment name is not a specification' and 'Choose a contract per purpose' (environment version, data provenance, identity model, network boundary, supported test types, cleanup owner). DevOps_Implementation_Handbook › DVO 13 › 'Write a compact environment contract'. QA_Environment_Handbook › QAE 06 › 'Choose a parity matrix' (identical / representative / intentionally different / unqualified). Development_Environment_Handbook › DEV 29 › 'Choose a claim based parity matrix'.
  - Recommendation: Give the environment record contract fields: purposes, a data rule (synthetic, masked, production copy forbidden), identity mode, provider simulation and its scenarios, claims established and not established, and a cleanup owner. Add a per-dimension parity reading against a chosen production environment. Let the stage select which checks apply, for example no standby requirement in Development. Change the Chapter 10 journey draft to one environment per purpose holding the whole system, so the draft does not raise errors by construction.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Confirmed. The environment record holds only title, purpose, owner, basis, stage, assetIds and confirmed (public/record-fields.js:190). No finding reads stage. I independently created clinic-appointments in memory (scratch/skeptic-ops/clinic.mjs) and got ENV-001 to ENV-006 with one asset each, 6 missing-dependency errors and 6 unscoped warnings. The per-component behaviour comes from the journey drafter (public/journey-drafter.js:433-450) committed through public/guided-creation.js:75, which adds one runtime.environment per asset. However, the grouped architecture drafter already does what the recommendation asks: one environment per stage holding every component (Production, Pre-production, plus Recovery when continuity or availability drivers exist), with the comment 'One set of environments (DRF-16), not one per component' (public/architecture-drafter.js:984-995). The fix is to align the journey path with the existing drafter, not to invent the approach. compareEnvironments is a partial design-level parity diff over 13 fields (public/architecture-analysis.js:43-47), but it has no dimension-based parity matrix.
- **[high | 2 Drivers / 10 Deployment / runtime | effort M] No service-level indicator definition or error budget**
  - AIW: Plan targets are a kind with operator, value, unit, window, basis and confirmation (public/driver-links.js:22-49). Chapter 2 has one target value per driver. There is no population or denominator, no reported-separately exclusions, no meaning for missing data, no time-based versus request-based budget, no burn-rate alert and no budget policy. A search of public/*.js found no 'error budget' or 'SLI' (only one SLO mention, in knowledge-pack.js).
  - Handbook: DevOps_Master › Chapter 08 › 'An objective needs a denominator', 'Distinguish time and request budgets', 'Work a burn rate example', 'Use budgets as decision support'. DevOps_Implementation_Handbook › DVO 22 › 'Show an objective record'. Banking_Production_Environment_Handbook › BPR 14 › 'Choose service level indicators' and 'Define exact measurement rules'.
  - Recommendation: Add an objective record linked to a Chapter 2 driver and a Chapter 10 plan. It would hold the indicator (population, success condition, measurement boundary), exclusions to report separately, the meaning of missing data, and a time-based or request-based window. It would also name the decision the objective drives: paging, release restriction or capacity action. Compute the allowed budget deterministically and show it as arithmetic, never as a score. Add warnings for an availability target without a population, and for a hard constraint treated as budgetable.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — The conclusion holds: there is no SLI record, denominator, budget arithmetic or burn-rate rule. Plan targets are kind, operator, value, unit, window and basis only (public/driver-links.js:22-49). The search evidence is wrong, though. The released governed catalogue contains PAT-ERROR-BUDGET (public/brain-catalogue.js:109) and PAT-SLO-BASED-OPERATIONS (public/brain-catalogue.js:253; also public/catalogue-index.js), both in the observability category. Both are offered by the Chapter 3 alternative 'Style, pattern, or tactic' picker (public/record-fields.js:131, :431-434). So an architect can name an error-budget or SLO-based-operations pattern as a Chapter 3 decision. AIW cannot model one.

- **[low | 3 Decisions / 10 | effort S] ADR handoff lacks revisit and supersession metadata, and runtime plans cannot cite decisions**
  - AIW: MADR export exists (public/output-documents.js:57). Superseding records a reason but has no supersededBy link. There is no review or revisit date, and there are no proposed, rejected or deferred states (aiw-map part 1 §3.9). SDD section 3 omits context, assumptions and risks (§3.9). A Chapter 10 plan reaches decisions only through its asset (aiw-map part 3 §10.3).
  - Handbook: Software_Application_Design_Handbook › SAD 04 › 'Record a concrete decision' and 'Handle change without rewriting history'. DevOps_Implementation_Handbook › DVO 03 › 'Write a decision that can be revisited'.
  - Recommendation: Add supersededBy and a revisit trigger or date to decisions, and print context and consequences in SDD section 3. Let Chapter 10 rollout, recovery and observability choices cite the Chapter 3 decision they realise, so the MADR set carries operability decisions to engineering.
  - Skeptic: HOLDS — Confirmed. Decision statuses are draft, recorded, accepted and superseded (public/decisions-domain.js:62). decision.supersede stores only a reason in history (public/decisions-domain.js:233-234), with no supersededBy link and no revisit date. A plan reaches decisions only through its asset; the register prints them under 'Decisions:' (public/runtime-domain.js:343).

- **[low | Cross-cutting (workbench library) / 10 | effort M] Reusable project-library runbooks and standards are approved by one person**
  - AIW: A library entry becomes 'approved' with a project source, a rationale and one person's reviewed tick (public/workbench-domain.js:183). An approved runbook can then rewrite a plan's runbook through a reviewed change (:71). That is sound for project guidance, but there is no four-eyes step and no organisation release, unlike governed knowledge claims (knowledge-governance.js:137-251).
  - Handbook: DevOps_Master › DOP-P023 'Governed self-service templates'. DevOps_Implementation_Handbook › DVO 24 › 'Verify the procedure as behavior'. Target_Platform_Qualification_and_Operational_Acceptance_Handbook › AS P12 'Keep one model lineage and separate approval'.
  - Recommendation: Keep project-library use as it is: reviewed, project-scoped and non-scoring. If runbooks or standards are to be reused across projects, route them through knowledge governance (source, claim, second-person review, signed organisation release) instead of library status. Never let candidate or discovery content enter the library path.
  - Skeptic: DOES NOT HOLD — Refuted. AIW already has the cross-project route the recommendation asks for. The organisation library accepts entry kinds including runbook and standard (organization-service.js:17). Its review requires a different authenticated reviewer: 'A different authenticated reviewer must assess this entry.' (organization-service.js:79-80). Approval needs stated conditions and limitations. A release requires independent review of the exact source and content hashes and is HMAC-signed (organization-service.js:85-87). Agents cannot review or release (worker.js:435). Adoption installs entries into the project library with the organisation signature (organization-service.js:90-97), and eligibility is checked again (organization-service.js:142-146). libraryCurrent then refuses an entry whose organisation signature or eligibility no longer holds (public/guidance-record.js:18). The single-person approval (public/workbench-domain.js:183) applies only to project-scoped entries, which is the intended design.
